```
1	# Todo
2	
3	This file uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains near term tasks with a short description and
5	uses links or reference links for more details.
6	
7	## In Progress
8	
9	_No cycle currently in progress._
10	
11	## Todo
12	
13	 Entries are in **strict priority rank** — #1 highest,
14	 descending. Reprioritize by moving an entry, then
15	 `vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/todo.md` to renumber.
16	 The numbers are positional rank, not stable IDs — to refer
17	 to a Todo, name it by its **title** (a greppable mention;
18	 a numbered list item has no anchor to link to), not its
19	 number. Long-tail entries
20	 live in [todo-backlog.md](todo-backlog.md). Use the
21	 [Prose Form in AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#prose-form); deeper
22	 detail goes in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` design
23	 subsections (link via `[N]` ref).
24	
25	1. Measure the wait-policy seam in iiac-perf: raw loop vs
26	   `_with(closure)` vs `_spin` must be zero-cost within
27	   noise before the endpoints build on it (the measurement
28	   clause of the shipped wait-policy hook) [[11]].
29	2. Descriptor queue endpoints: paired DescSender (loan +
30	   send) / DescReceiver (recv) owning ring endpoint +
31	   registry access, so the demo's ~20-line send path
32	   becomes ~3 lines and `resolve`'s unsafe is audited once
33	   inside the crate (recv safe by construction); guard
34	   handed back on Full [[11]].
35	3. Batch alloc/free demo: alongside the one-message
36	   alloc_free_1t loops, a variant that allocs X messages
37	   (5, 10, …) then frees them all, pool vs global
38	   allocator. We think the pool's rate stays constant
39	   (pop/push is O(1) regardless of live count, LIFO keeps
40	   the working set hot) while Box::new/drop slows as the
41	   batch outgrows malloc's thread-cache fast path — the
42	   demo should show it.
43	4. Endpoint claims word: CAS-claimed producer/consumer roles
44	   in the ring header so a second attach/split claimant gets
45	   an error instead of silently violating SPSC; costs a
46	   layout_version bump (or spends `_pad0`)
47	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#resolved-questions).
48	5. Typed endpoints: `Producer<T>` / `Consumer<T>` validating
49	   `T`'s geometry once at split instead of asserting on every
50	   reserve_slot_with [details](ring-buffer-design.md#api).
51	
52	## Ideas
53	
54	- Perf benches live in
55	  [iiac-perf](https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf)
56	  (sibling repo `../iiac-perf`), not here — its calibrated
57	  harness compares zc-ring against mpsc et al. directly
58	  (`zcring-1t`/`zcring-2t` mirroring `mpsc_1t`/`mpsc_2t`).
59	  An in-repo bench only if per-commit regression tracking
60	  proves necessary.
```

210	// commit -> reserve handoff, resolved exactly once.
211	let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }?;
212	//                  ... read msg ...
213	msg.free();                           // buffer back to its pool
214	```
215	
216	One allocation's bytes are written once and never copied —
217	not by send, not by receive. (`resolve` is the one `unsafe`:
218	validation rejects unknown ids / bad indices / wrong types,
219	but ownership uniqueness is the caller's promise. Paired
220	sender/receiver endpoints that encapsulate it — and shrink
221	this to loan / send / recv — are the next cycle; see
222	notes/todo.md.)
223	
224	**Trust.** Unchanged from the ring: every word in shared
225	memory is untrusted input. The pool validates the head and
226	next-link at every pop and fails toward `Exhausted`; a
227	hostile peer can degrade service (garbage messages, lost
228	buffers, spurious exhaustion), never cause UB on this side.
229

67	- `#[global_allocator]` experiment over size-class pools:
68	  GlobalAlloc is `&self` + any-thread, so it needs
69	  shared-allocation pools (phase 2 gen-tagged head) or
70	  per-thread pools with a routing layer; arbitrary `Layout`
71	  needs size-class selection + an oversize fallback. Frees
72	  from any thread are already natural (MPSC push). Classic
73	  mempool → malloc arc; measure the object-pool form in
74	  iiac-perf first.
75	- Private per-handle cache in front of the shared free-stack
76	  (tcache-over-arenas): alloc/free hit a thread-private list
77	  with plain load/store; refill/flush moves batches to the
78	  CAS stack, amortizing one CAS over N messages. Motivating
79	  datum: 2 uncontended CAS = 8.7 ns of the pool's 9.9 ns
80	  single-thread round trip (vs malloc tcache's zero
81	  atomics). Hold until iiac-perf shows per-op CAS matters in
82	  a composed workload; we think the pool's tail latency
83	  (p99, stddev) already beats malloc — no arena locks, no
84	  brk/mmap — and that matters more than the mean.
85	- `Message` trait over the payload cast boilerplate: const
86	  `MSG_ID` + the zerocopy bounds; receiver-side dispatch
87	  (read tag, match, cast) without per-call-site ceremony,
88	  and maybe a transport seam so an embedded
89	  pointer-descriptor profile slots in behind the same API
90	  [details](ring-buffer-design.md#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).
91	- BufSlot auto-free on Drop (RAII, iceoryx2-style): kills the
92	  silent leak-on-drop footgun at the cost of guard-type
93	  asymmetry (ring guards' drop = do-nothing) and a
94	  ManuallyDrop dance in free/send paths. Decide when
95	  descriptor-queue send lands — explicit free is easier to
96	  upgrade than to walk back.
97	- Overflow FIFO: when a queue's ring is Full, append the
98	  message to a sender-private intrusive pending list (same
99	  embedded next-link the free-stack uses; zero allocation,
100	  naturally bounded by pool capacity)
101	  [details](ring-buffer-design.md#overflow-fifo-future).
102	- Blocking layer above the crate (futex, eventfd, async

114	- Per-target / configurable `CACHE_LINE` (128 for Apple
115	  M-series false sharing, tiny for cache-less MCUs) — safe

416	### Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies
417	
418	Design captured from the post-demo review; promoted to
419	`## Todo` #1 and #2.
420	
421	- The demo's send path is ~20 lines of ceremony. Target
422	  shape is ~3: `loan` → populate → `send`. Paired
423	  `DescSender` (ring producer + pool + registry access;
424	  `loan` carries the single-popper token structurally) and
425	  `DescReceiver` (consumer + registry). The deep win is
426	  safety, not line count: descriptors entering the ring
427	  only through the paired sender make `recv` satisfy
428	  resolve's contract by construction — the `unsafe` gets
429	  audited once inside the crate instead of at every call
430	  site. iceoryx2's loan/send shape, as the design doc's
431	  prior-art note anticipated.
432	- Wait policies as an injected closure: `_with(impl
433	  FnMut(u32) -> bool)` — attempt count in, keep-waiting
434	  out — uniformly over the three wait points (Exhausted /
435	  Full / Empty). Tiers: non-blocking primitive (unchanged,
436	  the only tier with protocol knowledge), the `_with`
437	  hook, shipped policies we dogfood (`_spin`) as the model
438	  users copy, and user compositions (their own
439	  `get::<Msg>()`, policy baked in, no closure in their
440	  signature). Polling only — true blocking (futex,
441	  eventfd) needs a peer wake over the user line and stays
442	  a separate layer.
443	- Validation order: the wait-policy hook lands first and
444	  iiac-perf measures raw loop vs closure vs `_spin` — the
445	  seam must be zero-cost before the endpoints build on it.
446

50	- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
51	  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
52	  this project follows the per-commit preference.
53	
54	## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)
55	
56	Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
57	on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
58	the exact commit it came from.
59	
60	- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
61	  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
62	  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
63	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
64	  if it differs.
65	- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
66	  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
67	  contain (see the cycle protocol's
68	  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).
69	
70	## Step numbering
71	
72	The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
73	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
74	phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
75	a Preparation**:
76	
77	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
78	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
79	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
80	
81	**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
82	no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
83	Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
84	needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
85	grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
86	renumbering siblings.
87	
88	Disambiguation:
89	
90	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
91	  Preparation.
92	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
93	  identifier `0`).
94	
95	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
96	
97	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
98	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
99	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
100	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
101	
102	Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
103	active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
104	every commit carries a distinct version.
105	
```

551	## Performance runs using becnches in iiac-perf
552	
553	Commits: [[32]]
554	
555	300s (5 min) iiac-perf round-trip runs recorded for the ring's
556	three wait forms, which surfaced bug #1: the `raw`
557	loop-on-`reserve_slot` pattern reads both indices every spin,
558	while `_with` / `_spin` hoist the caller-owned index out of the
559	loop — measurably slower and jitterier at 2t.
560	
561	- README.md gained the zcr-{raw,with,spin}-{1t,2t} benchmark
562	  blocks from those runs.
563	- notes/bugs.md filed bug #1 (drop the dominated `reserve_slot`
564	  rung), which the "refactor: drop reserve_slot, keep _with
565	  ladder" cycle below resolves.
566	- The `becnches` misspelling in the header is Wink's, kept
567	  verbatim from the commit title so the section is an exact
568	  `git log --grep` match — not a chores-side typo.
569	
570	## refactor: drop reserve_slot, keep _with ladder
571	
572	Commits: [[33]]
573	
574	`reserve_slot` on both endpoints was a strictly dominated API
575	rung: `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` does everything it did
576	with identical loads, and its naive loop-on-the-fallible-call
577	usage carried a measured +30 ns / ~2× body jitter under
578	cross-core traffic (bug #1, whose raw-pattern benches live in
579	iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-*`). Removing it leaves a two-rung ladder
580	per endpoint — `reserve_slot_with` (fallible primitive) +
581	`reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience).
582	
583	- `reserve_slot_with` is now the self-contained primitive: its
584	  doc absorbed the single-probe (`|_| false`) note and the
585	  raw-pointer / `>=`-corruption rationale the deleted method
586	  used to host.
587	- Call sites split by intent: single-probe (the `_1t` demos,
588	  the assertion tests, the README examples) →
589	  `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`; loop-on-fallible spin waits
590	  (the `_2t` demos, the registry tests) → `reserve_slot_spin`.
591	- `threaded_spsc` keeps looping on `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`
592	  so the fallible primitive still has a threaded-contention
593	  test distinct from `threaded_spsc_spin_variants` (which
594	  covers the spin rung).
595	- No `try_reserve_slot` alias yet: nothing outside tests makes
596	  `_with(|_| false)` common, so per bug #1 the alias waits for
597	  a real consumer.
598	
599	### ring-buffer-design.md: blocking intro reconciled
600

```
vc-x1 0.67.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
```

```
## Memory layout

One contiguous `#[repr(C)]` region, mappable into multiple
address spaces: a `Header` struct followed by the slot array.
The `Header` spans four cache lines; each concurrently-written
index owns its own line, and alignment is expressed on exactly
the fields that need it via a `CacheAligned` wrapper (the
compiler computes the inter-line padding):

```rust
/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole
/// ownership of the line.
#[repr(C, align(64))]
struct CacheAligned<T>(T);

#[repr(C)]
struct Header {
    // line 0 — geometry; written by init (magic last), then
    // read-only. Cold: per-op paths use handle snapshots.
    magic: AtomicU32,          // layout marker; init/attach handshake
    layout_version: AtomicU32, // bumped on any layout change
    slot_size: AtomicU32,      // N in bytes, cache-line multiple
    capacity: AtomicU32,       // M, power of two; index mask is M - 1
    cache_line_size: AtomicU32, // CACHE_LINE_SIZE the region was built with
    // line 1 — producer-owned
    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>, // written by producer only
    // line 2 — consumer-owned
    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>, // written by consumer only
    // line 3 — app-owned scratch; zeroed at init, never
    // touched by the crate again
    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; 16]>,
}
```

```
offset 0                    Header (256 bytes, 4 cache lines)
offset size_of::<Header>()  slots: M × N bytes
```

- One type owns the whole control-block layout:
  `size_of::<Header>()` is the slot-array offset.
- **User line last, on purpose** — an app-side overrun walking
  forward out of `user` lands in slot 0 (the app's own message
  data), not in an index line.
- **`repr(align(N))` takes only an integer literal** — it
  cannot name `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, so the `64` is written out and
  const asserts tie `align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>()`
  and `size_of::<Header>()` back to the constant.
- **Every field is atomic** — the region may be mapped by a
  peer at any time, so even "immutable" geometry must be free
  of data races; a peer scribbling on plain fields would be
  UB, on atomics it is merely garbage values. `AtomicU32` has
  the same layout as `u32`, so layout_version is unaffected.
- **Init/attach handshake** — init stores the geometry and
  indices first (`Relaxed`), then `magic` **last** with
  `Release`; attach loads `magic` **first** with `Acquire`. A
  peer that pre-mapped the region and observes MAGIC therefore
  also observes the geometry it validates. (Re-initializing a
  region a peer is already attached to stays outside the
  contract.)
- **Slots** — `M` slots of `N` bytes, each cache-line
  aligned (N is a line multiple, so alignment follows from
  the array base).
- **`CACHE_LINE_SIZE = 64` plays two roles** — a layout-ABI
  constant and a false-sharing pad. On CPUs with 128-byte
  effective line pitch (Apple M-series; Intel's adjacent-line
  prefetcher is why crossbeam pads 128 on x86_64 too) the
  padding under-separates — a perf question for the bench
  work, not correctness. Since layout v2 the header records
  the build's `cache_line_size` and attach rejects a mismatch
  (`BadCacheLine`), so the constant can become per-target
  (cfg) or shrink for cache-less embedded tiers without any
  silent cross-build corruption.
- **Assume nothing beyond `AtomicU32` load/store** — the
  protocol uses no CAS/RMW, so it reaches
  load/store-only targets (e.g. `thumbv6m`/Cortex-M0).
  Features that want CAS (endpoint claims) should degrade or
  gate rather than raise the floor. 8/16-bit targets (AVR,
  MSP430 — no `AtomicU32`) would need index-width
  genericization; out of scope, recorded in Ideas.

Index scheme (resolves the atomic-width question):

- **Fixed-width `AtomicU32`, free-running**:
  - increments are wrapping adds, never masked in storage
  - fixed width keeps the layout identical for 32- and
    64-bit peers sharing the region
- **Masked only at slot access**:
  - slot position = `idx & (M - 1)`
- **Occupancy** = `producer_idx.wrapping_sub(consumer_idx)`
  (valid while `M <= 2^31`):
  - empty: `producer_idx == consumer_idx`
  - full: `producer_idx - consumer_idx == M` — implemented as
    `>= M`, so a peer-corrupted consumer_idx fails toward
    `Full` instead of handing out an unowned slot
  - no sacrificial slot
- **u32 wrap at `2^32` is harmless** because M is a power
  of two:
  - `2^32 % M == 0`, so occupancy math and masked slot
    positions both carry across the wrap unchanged
- **Example** (M = 4, `p` = producer_idx, `c` = consumer_idx):
  - start: `p = 0, c = 0` — occupancy 0, empty
  - producer commits 4 messages: `p = 4, c = 0` — occupancy
    4 == M, full; slot positions written were 0, 1, 2, 3
  - consumer releases 1: `p = 4, c = 1` — occupancy 3, one
    slot free; the next reservation writes slot `4 & 3 == 0`

Ordering: the producer loads `consumer_idx` with `Acquire`
and publishes `producer_idx` with `Release`; the consumer
mirrors (loads `producer_idx` `Acquire`, publishes
`consumer_idx` `Release`).

## API
```

```
## API

Construction (resolves the foreign-memory question):

- `Ring::init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:
  u32) -> Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates geometry
  (power-of-two M, line-multiple N, alignment, region big
  enough — sized in u64, since the N×M product can wrap a
  32-bit usize), then writes the header per the
  [handshake](#memory-layout) (indices zeroed, magic last).
- `unsafe Ring::attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) ->
  Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates magic (Acquire),
  layout_version, and geometry against the region length,
  over the same shared `header_ptr` checks as init. Unsafe,
  deliberately, on two grounds:
  - zerocopy's shared validated casts require `Immutable`,
    which `Header` correctly is not (atomics) — so the cast
    is hand-verified rather than derive-verified;
  - only the caller can vouch that the pointer is a live,
    genuinely shared, writable mapping (e.g. `MAP_SHARED`)
    and that the SPSC role contract holds across processes.
- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — consumes
  the `Ring`, so each handle exists at most once per ring per
  process (SPSC discipline; cross-process there is one
  producer process and one consumer process by contract).
- **Geometry snapshot** — slot_size / capacity / mask are
  copied into `Ring` and the handles at init/attach; per-op
  paths never re-read peer-writable header fields. Combined
  with the all-atomic header, a hostile peer can degrade the
  ring to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty — never UB.

Producer — reserve_slot_with/commit, in place:

- `producer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_full) ->
  Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>` — next free slot as `&mut T`
  (zerocopy `FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout`), retrying
  under the injected wait policy — `on_full(attempt) -> bool`,
  `|_| false` for a single non-blocking probe. `T` geometry is
  asserted against the slot size on each call (a mismatch is a
  programming error, so it panics; typed endpoints that check
  once are a follow-on — see [todo.md](todo.md)).
- `WriteSlot::commit(self)` — publishes the slot
  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit
  abandons the reservation (nothing published).

Consumer — reserve_slot_with/release, in place:

- `consumer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_empty) ->
  Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>` — oldest unread slot as
  `&T`, retrying under the injected wait policy (`|_| false`
  for a single non-blocking probe).
- `ReadSlot::release(self)` — frees the slot
  (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without release
  leaves the slot unread — the next reservation returns it
  again.

Each side reserves at most **one slot at a time**: the guard
holds the endpoint's `&mut` borrow, so a second reservation
while a guard is live is a compile error, not a runtime state.

Both endpoints also expose `user() -> &[AtomicU32; 16]` — the
header's app-owned scratch line; see
[Blocking and user words](#blocking-and-user-words).

Guard internals: both guards hold **raw slot pointers** and
mint `&T` / `&mut T` per access via Deref. A reference *field*
would be argument-protected (noalias) for the entire
`commit`/`release` call, but the store inside that call hands
the slot to the other side, which may access it while the
protector is still live — an aliasing race Miri flags
(found in 0.3.0-6 by the threaded test).

Batch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —
the guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the
single-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.

### System model (overview)

The layer's rules at a glance; each bullet links to the
section that expands it.

- A process may have one or more pools
  [details](#pool-topology-and-phasing).
  - Each pool has one owning allocator: a single thread
    allocates, any holder frees.
- Not all pools in a process need to be in shared memory
  [details](#layer-requirements).
  - A process-private pool serves intra-process messaging.
- A message to be sent to another process must come from a
  pool in shared memory
  [details](#pool-id-resolution).
  - The receiving process must have that pool mapped.
- Queues carry descriptors, not payloads
  [details](#descriptors).
  - A descriptor is a uniform small
    `(pool id, buffer index)` regardless of message size.
  - The payload stays at rest in its pool buffer.
- Any message travels over any queue
  [details](#layer-requirements).
  - Precondition: both endpoints have the message's pool
    registered.
- Each process keeps its own pool registry
  [details](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).
  - It maps pool ids to that process's view (mapping) of
    each pool.
  - Registries are private and never shared; only
    descriptors cross process boundaries.
  - Pool ids are the shared vocabulary: every participating
    process must register a pool under the same id (the
    assignment scheme is an open question —
    [details](#pool-id-allocation)).
- Ownership follows the descriptor
  [details](#usage-model-roles-and-buffer-lifecycle).
  - alloc → write → send the descriptor over a queue →
    the receiver owns it → read, forward over another
    queue, or free.
  - A buffer is always in exactly one state with one
    permitted toucher.
- Any holder — any thread or process — may free
  [details](#free-path-intrusive-lifo-free-stack).
  - The free pushes the buffer directly onto its pool's
    free-stack in shared memory — no round-trip through
    the owning process.
  - The owning allocator finds the buffer on a later
    alloc.
- Descriptors and free-stack links arriving through shared
  memory are untrusted
  [details](#trust).
  - Both are validated before use.
  - Corruption degrades to errors or lost buffers, never
    UB.
- 0.7.0 builds the in-process slice of this model
  [details](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).
  - As built, everything is single-process: one address
    space, threads as the peers, no shared-memory mapping
    code yet.
  - Cross-process setup (mapping exchange, pool-id
    coordination) stays design
    [details](#open-questions).

### Layer requirements

- **Decoupled get/hold/send/free** — allocate a message, hold
  it indefinitely, send it over any queue later, forward it,
  or free it unsent.
- **Any quantity of queues and pools** in a system, and any
  message can travel over any queue.
- **Inter- and intra-process** — processes share queue ends,
  and message payloads zero-copy across process boundaries.
  The embedded single-address-space case is the degenerate
  mapping where all peers share one base.
- **Heterogeneous pools** — pools differ arbitrarily in buffer
  size and count; each meets the alignment constraints and
  self-describes its geometry.
- **Phasing** — queues are SPSC rings now, an MPSC ring is a
  future sibling primitive; pools have a single allocator
  now, shared allocation eventually.

### Free path: intrusive LIFO free-stack

Freeing must be cheap for many freers and allocation must be
O(1) with no searching (a per-buffer available-flag makes free
trivial but alloc a scan). The free operation is itself the
marking: push the buffer onto the pool's free-stack.

- **Intrusive** — a freed buffer's header holds the next-link
  as an offset; the free-list needs no extra storage.
- **LIFO on purpose** — we think a stack beats a free-ring on
  cache behavior: FIFO cycles through every buffer (maximum
  working set), LIFO reuses the most-recently-freed few, and
  the small working set also helps the TLB. The full
  same-thread malloc-style hot-reuse win is diluted because
  the freer and allocator are different cores; the
  working-set effect stands regardless.
- **Single-popper Treiber stack** — free = CAS the head to
  your buffer's offset (MPSC push side: any thread or process
  frees into any pool); alloc = the one owning allocator pops
  the head. A single popper eliminates classic Treiber ABA:
  no node leaves the list under a competing pop, pushes just
  retry the CAS.
- **Validated pops** — head and next-links are untrusted;
  the popper bounds/alignment-checks each offset and caps
  pops per attempt, so a corrupt peer can lose buffers or
  cycle the list but never make the allocator read outside
  the pool.
- **CAS note** — the free-stack is the first structure to
  need CAS; the ring protocol itself stays load/store-only,
  so the atomic floor rises only for pool users.

### Pool topology and phasing

- **One owning allocator per pool** (invariant, phase 1) — a
  single thread allocates; any party frees. Per-thread pools
  are the performance default and satisfy this trivially.
- **Shared pools already exist in phase 1** — shared for
  reading, forwarding, and freeing; only allocation is
  single-owner.
- **Shared allocation (phase 2)** — per-thread pools cost
  memory; N threads sharing one pool's capacity needs
  multi-popper alloc, which needs an ABA-proof head:
  pack `(offset, generation)` in one `AtomicU64` and CAS
  both together. A contained per-pool variant (opt-in flag),
  not a redesign; hot paths keep the cheap single-popper
  version. Heterogeneity mitigates meanwhile: a small private
  hot-path pool plus a big shared fallback pool.
- **MPSC ring (future sibling)** — multi-producer queues need
  a different ring protocol (CAS-claimed producer index,
  per-slot sequence state); it slots in as a sibling
  primitive under the same descriptor layer. The endpoint
  claims word should model role slots, not a single producer
  bit, so N producer claims fit later without a layout
  rethink.

### Usage model: roles and buffer lifecycle
====
### Open questions

One question per heading, each directly linkable; a
resolved question migrates to
[Resolved questions](#resolved-questions), keeping its
heading (and so its anchor).

#### Pool-id allocation

In-process settled in 0.7.0 (registry slot index, no
unregister); cross-process remains open:
coordinator-assigned versus derived from the shm object's
identity.

#### Setup plane

How a process discovers and maps a pool it hasn't seen:
pre-arranged at init (embedded-friendly, allocation-free
after startup) versus fd-passing over a Unix socket
(Linux-friendly, dynamic); likely both, as profiles.

#### Message header shape

0.7.0 settled the near half: provenance travels in the
descriptor, a type tag is payload-level, and the in-buffer
header is deferred (see
[Descriptor and registry design](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070)).
Still open: whether a length (or anything else) joins a
future in-buffer header, and its layout against the
embedded next-link — the link is load-bearing for three
states (free-stack, pending FIFO, future lists), so they
must be laid out together when that header lands.

## Resolved questions

- **Cross-process trust** — resolved in 0.3.0-4: the
  all-atomic header plus the geometry snapshot mean a hostile
  peer cannot cause UB, only garbage messages (still valid
  `T`s — zerocopy guarantees representation, not sense) or
  spurious Full/Empty. `attach` remains `unsafe` for the
  mapping-liveness contract, not for peer trust.
- **`split()` once-guard** — direction chosen: an
  endpoint-claims word in the header (CAS to claim the
  producer / consumer role, second claimant gets an error),
  which also catches in-process double-attach; costs a
  layout_version bump (or spends `_pad0`). Promoted to a
  `## Todo` entry in [todo.md](todo.md); the documented
  contract stands until then.

1	//! Producing endpoint: [`Producer`] reserves the next free
2	//! slot, the [`WriteSlot`] guard writes it in place and
3	//! commits.
4	
5	use core::marker::PhantomData;
6	use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
7	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
8	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
9	
10	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
11	
12	/// `reserve_slot_with` failed: every slot holds an
13	/// uncommitted-or-unread message.
14	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
15	pub struct Full;
16	
17	/// The producing endpoint: `reserve_slot_with`, write in
18	/// place, `commit`.
19	pub struct Producer<'a> {
20	    /// The ring's control block.
21	    header: &'a Header,
22	    /// Base of the slot array.
23	    slots: *mut u8,
24	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
25	    slot_size: u32,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
27	    capacity: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
31	}
32	
33	// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it
34	// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off
35	// with Release/Acquire ordering.
36	unsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}
37	
38	impl<'a> Producer<'a> {
39	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
40	    /// geometry snapshot.
41	    pub(crate) fn new(
42	        header: &'a Header,
43	        slots: *mut u8,
44	        slot_size: u32,
45	        capacity: u32,
46	        mask: u32,
47	    ) -> Self {
48	        Producer {
49	            header,
50	            slots,
51	            slot_size,
52	            capacity,
53	            mask,
54	            _region: PhantomData,
55	        }
56	    }
57	
58	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
59	    /// never touched by the crate again.
60	    ///
61	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
62	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
63	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
64	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
65	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
66	        &self.header.user
67	    }
68	
69	    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, applying an
70	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or
71	    /// the policy gives up → [`Full`].
72	    ///
73	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
74	    ///   holds the `&mut Producer` borrow, so a second
75	    ///   reservation before the guard is dropped or committed
76	    ///   does not compile.
77	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`WriteSlot::commit`]
78	    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).
79	    /// - `on_full` is called after each failed attempt with
80	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
81	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`. Pass `|_| false`
82	    ///   for a single non-blocking probe.
83	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
84	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying could not return
85	    ///   it): `producer_idx` is ours and loaded once; only
86	    ///   `consumer_idx` is re-read per attempt.
87	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
88	    ///   and the composition model.
89	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
90	        &mut self,
91	        mut on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
92	    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
93	    where
94	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
95	    {
96	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
97	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
98	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
99	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
100	        loop {
101	            // `>=`, not `==`: a peer-corrupted consumer_idx
102	            // makes occupancy look huge — fail Full, never hand
103	            // out a slot the protocol doesn't own.
104	            let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
105	            if p.wrapping_sub(c) < self.capacity {
106	                break;
107	            }
108	            if !on_full(attempt) {
109	                return Err(Full);
110	            }
111	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
112	        }
113	        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T`: a reference field would be
114	        // argument-protected for the whole `commit(self)` call,
115	        // while commit's Release store lets the consumer read
116	        // the slot inside that window — UB (Miri-verified).
117	        // Deref mints short-lived references instead.
118	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
119	        Ok(WriteSlot {
120	            header: self.header,
121	            msg,
122	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
123	            _slot: PhantomData,
124	        })
125	    }
126	}
127	
128	/// A reserved write slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then
129	/// [`commit`](WriteSlot::commit).
130	pub struct WriteSlot<'p, T> {
131	    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).
132	    header: &'p Header,
133	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
134	    /// see the comment in [`Producer::reserve_slot_with`].
135	    msg: *mut T,
136	    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.
137	    next_idx: u32,
138	    /// Owns the `&'p mut` borrow of the producer.
139	    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,
140	}
141	
142	impl<T> Deref for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
143	    type Target = T;
144	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
145	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
146	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
147	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
148	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot_with), and the index
149	        // protocol gives this guard exclusive slot access until
150	        // commit.
151	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
152	    }
153	}
154	
155	impl<T> DerefMut for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
156	    /// Write access to the in-slot message.
157	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
158	        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of
159	        // the minted reference.
160	        unsafe { &mut *self.msg }
161	    }
162	}
163	
164	impl<T> WriteSlot<'_, T> {
165	    /// Publish the slot to the consumer
166	    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
167	    pub fn commit(self) {
168	        self.header
169	            .producer_idx
170	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
171	    }
172	}
173	
```

> AGENT

I've got the full picture — and helpfully, the design doc already reserves a seat for this: [Pool topology and phasing](notes/ring-buffer-design.md) says *"**MPSC ring (future sibling)** — multi-producer queues need a different ring protocol (CAS-claimed producer index, per-slot sequence state); it slots in as a sibling primitive under the same descriptor layer."* So "separate API" isn't just a measurement convenience — it's the already-recorded architecture. Here are my thoughts, then a cycle plan.

## Why sibling, not a mode (agreeing with you, with teeth)

- The SPSC hot path is exactly **one relaxed load + one acquire load + one release store** per side, no CAS, no per-slot state. That's what buys the 1.9 ns adjusted 1t round-trip you just pasted. Any unified type (runtime mode flag, generic over "claim strategy") puts at least a branch — realistically a per-slot sequence check — into that path.
- Separate types also keep the **atomic floor** story clean: the SPSC protocol stays load/store-only (thumbv6m works today); MPSC needs CAS, so its module gets gated (`cfg(target_has_atomic = "32")`) and only its users pay. Same precedent as the pool's Treiber free-stack — CAS entered the crate there without raising the ring's floor.
- And it gives you the A/B you want: same workload, same harness, two primitives.

## Protocol sketch (Vyukov-style, restricted to MP+SC)

- **Per-slot sequence array** between header and slots (`M × AtomicU32`). Slot at position `pos` is claimable when `seq == pos`, committed when `seq == pos + 1`, released by the consumer storing `seq = pos + M`.
- **Producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`**: load seq at `p & mask`; if ready, `compare_exchange_weak(p, p+1)`; on success the slot is exclusively yours — write payload, then `seq.store(p + 1, Release)` publishes it. Claim order ≠ commit order is exactly why the per-slot seq exists.
- **Consumer stays load/store** on its side: single consumer means its index is private — wait for `seq == c + 1` (Acquire), read, store `seq = c + M` (Release), advance. No CAS on the consume path.
- **Producers never read `consumer_idx`** — fullness falls out of the slot's seq (`seq < p` ⇒ still unreleased ⇒ full). The header keeps a consumer_idx line only for occupancy/diagnostics, if at all.
- **u32 free-running + power-of-two M** carries over unchanged; wrap math is the same.
- **Own magic + layout_version** — a region is one kind or the other; cross-attach fails toward `BadMagic`, no shared-layout entanglement.
- **Trust:** seq words are shared memory ⇒ untrusted. Positions are always masked, so a hostile peer scribbling seqs yields spurious Full/Empty or garbage-but-valid `T`s — the established degrade-never-UB posture holds. Needs its own written analysis in the design doc.

## The one genuinely new API problem: abandoned claims

In SPSC, dropping a `WriteSlot` without commit is free — `producer_idx` never moved. In MPSC, a claimed-but-never-committed index **wedges the queue**: the consumer waits at that position forever. Two candidate shapes:

1. **Closure send** — `producer.send_with::<T>(on_full, |msg: &mut T| …)`: commit happens by construction on closure return; abandonment is unrepresentable. Cost: panic-in-closure needs an answer (tombstone on unwind, or document abort).
2. **Guard + tombstone** — keep the `reserve/commit` guard mirror; `Drop` without commit stores a skip marker (e.g. a reserved seq bit) the consumer releases without delivering.

My lean: **closure send as the primary API, tombstone as the unwind path** — you need the tombstone machinery for panic safety anyway, so the guard form could even come later if wanted. Symmetry with SPSC's guard is nice but not worth a footgun.

## Measurement plan (the point of the separation)

iiac-perf side (sibling repo), mirroring the `zcr` set:

- `zcr-mpsc-1t` — same-thread round-trip: pure protocol overhead (CAS + seq vs load/store), zero contention. Directly against `zcr-with-1t`'s 1.9 ns.
- `zcr-mpsc-2t` — 1 producer / 1 consumer cross-core: apples-to-apples with `zcr-with-2t`'s ~98 ns; the "what does MPSC cost when you don't need it" number.
- `zcr-mpsc-3t` (2p/1c), maybe 5t (4p/1c) — contention scaling, the number SPSC can't produce.
- Worth one more comparator: **fan-in of N SPSC rings** (one ring per producer, consumer polls) — the load/store-only alternative an MPSC ring has to beat or at least justify against. Cheap to bench since SPSC already exists.

One open perf question to carry into the benches: seq array **packed** (Vyukov default, adjacent-slot false sharing) vs **line-padded** (M×64 bytes overhead). Start packed, measure.

## Cycle ladder sketch

- `-0` Preparation: expand the "future sibling" bullet into a full `### MPSC ring design` section (protocol, layout, API + abandoned-claim resolution, trust analysis); todo entry.
- `-1` feat: MpscRing layout + init/attach + validation (magic, seq array, geometry) + unit tests.
- `-2` feat: producer claim/commit + consumer + threaded stress + Miri.
- `-3` feat: demo scoreboard lines (mpsc flows at the existing placements).
- `-4` (iiac-perf repo): `zcr-mpsc-*` benches + README numbers here.
- Close-out.

Interactions to note: the **endpoint claims word** todo already says to model *role slots, not a single producer bit*, precisely so N producer claims fit later — this plan validates that. And the eventual payoff is at the descriptor layer: N senders sharing one queue to a receiver, which is where "MPSC capabilities" really earns its keep.

Three questions before I write anything down:

1. **API shape** — closure-send primary (my lean), guard+tombstone primary, or both from the start?
2. **Priority** — does this become the new Todo #1, ahead of descriptor queue endpoints? (Endpoints would then plausibly grow an MPSC variant later, so order matters.)
3. Include the **fan-in-SPSC comparator** in the bench matrix, or keep the first pass minimal?

### Overflow FIFO (future)

When a queue's ring is Full, the sender appends the message to
a pending FIFO instead of failing — "send" then always
succeeds while pool memory lasts.

- **Intrusive, zero-allocation** — the FIFO links through the
  same embedded next-link offset the free-stack uses. A
  buffer is on at most one list at a time (free stack,
  pending FIFO, in flight in a ring, held by an owner), so
  one link field serves every state.
- **Sender-private** — the pending list belongs to the
  producer endpoint: head + tail offsets for O(1) append, no
  shared mutation, no CAS. Order discipline: the producer
  drains the FIFO oldest-first before ring-sending anything
  new, or FIFO order breaks. Draining happens on subsequent
  send attempts and/or an explicit flush.
- **Validated traversal** — the links live in shared pool
  memory a peer can scribble; the drainer bounds- and
  alignment-checks each offset as the free-stack popper does.
- **Naturally bounded** — messages come from pools, so the
  FIFO cannot outgrow total pool capacity; backpressure
  reappears as allocation failure rather than queue Full.

### Prior art: iceoryx2

> DEVELOPER

I'm think MPSC is next but I think we already are using a notion of descriptors the a slot in the ring is just an array it's definition is defined by the user. Initialy the contents was the buffer itself and then we created Pool for the "message" alloc/free and a descriptor is the "pointer" (pool id + offset) to the message in shared memory. So we're using a descriptor now, but maybe the one in todo 1 is different.

> AGENT

You're right that descriptors already exist — and your history is exactly what the repo records. The clarification is that Todo #1 is *not* a new descriptor notion; it's a wrapper layer over the machinery you already have:

- **What exists today (landed 0.7.0, "feat: descriptor queues over the SPSC ring"):** `Desc` (pool id + buffer index), the per-process `Registry`, and the pattern of sending `Desc` over a ring whose slot type is just `Desc`. The ring never learned about descriptors — a slot is still user-defined bytes, as you say; `Desc` is merely a small `T`.
- **What Todo #1 adds:** paired **endpoint types** — `DescSender` (owns ring producer + pool + registry access; `loan` → populate → `send`) and `DescReceiver` (consumer + registry; `recv`). Two wins:
  - **Ergonomics** — the demo's ~20-line send ceremony (alloc, write, reserve slot, copy desc, commit) becomes ~3 lines.
  - **Safety** — today every receive site calls `unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }` and personally vouches for ownership uniqueness (README line 211 is exactly this). If descriptors can only *enter* the ring through the paired sender, then `recv` satisfies resolve's contract **by construction** — the `unsafe` gets audited once inside the crate, and the user-facing receive path is safe.

So same descriptors, new clothing. No conflict with MPSC-first — if anything the order helps: when the endpoints work comes after, `DescSender` can be designed against both ring flavors from day one (and it's also where each sender's private overflow FIFO will live, per our earlier discussion).

That settles priority: **MPSC ring becomes Todo #1**, descriptor queue endpoints slip to #2 with a note that they'll cover both flavors.

Last open item: the bench matrix. My recommendation stands — include the **fan-in-of-N-SPSC-rings comparator** (one ring per producer, consumer polls round-robin). It's nearly free to build since SPSC exists, and it's the honest baseline: if fan-in beats the CAS ring at every producer count, that's a result worth knowing *before* building endpoints on the MPSC ring. Minimal pass is fine too if you'd rather see the raw mpsc numbers first.

Say the word on fan-in (in or later), and I'll write the `-0` Preparation: the `### MPSC ring design` section in ring-buffer-design.md (protocol, layout, closure-send API, overflow readiness, trust), the todo reshuffle, and the chores section — then commit.

> DEVELOPER

Sorry explain "fan-in"? But maybe you talking about this; I believe there are two methods for creating a MPSC, one is each producer has it own "queue" and the consumer has N endpoints, i.e. basically an SPSC for each producer. The other notion is one "queue" and that all producers share. Is that what you mean or am I totally confused? I definitely could be :)

> AGENT

You're not confused at all — that's exactly it. The two architectures:

1. **Fan-in of SPSC rings** — each producer gets its own SPSC ring; the one consumer holds N consumer endpoints and polls across them ("fans them in"). Buildable *today* with shipped primitives, and it stays load/store-only — no CAS anywhere.
2. **Shared MPSC ring** — one ring, all producers CAS-claim slots in it. The new sibling primitive we're planning.

The trade-offs are real on both sides, which is why measuring them against each other is interesting:

| | Fan-in (N × SPSC) | Shared MPSC ring |
|---|---|---|
| Atomics | load/store only | CAS on claim (contended under load) |
| Producer↔producer interference | none — disjoint rings | claim contention, shared `producer_idx` line |
| Consumer cost | polls N rings (idle latency and fairness scale with N) | polls one place |
| Memory | N full rings | one ring + seq array |
| Adding a producer | new ring + consumer must learn of it (awkward membership) | just another claimant |
| Ordering | per-producer FIFO (same as MPSC promises) | per-producer FIFO |

So "include the fan-in comparator" means: alongside `zcr-mpsc-{1t,2t,3t}`, also bench fan-in at the same producer counts. If the poll-N-rings design beats the CAS ring at realistic N, that's cheap to learn now and expensive to learn after building endpoints on the wrong primitive. My lean remains to include it — the SPSC pieces already exist, so it's mostly bench-harness work in iiac-perf.

(There are also hybrid designs — e.g. per-producer rings with a shared wakeup word — but that's composition on top of these two, not a third primitive.)

Include fan-in in the bench matrix, or minimal pass first?

IMO both have their place and we'll likely allow both architectures, but we'll see.

I've desgined similar systems and there both (and hybrids) have there place. With a single shared queue is "fair FIFO" order with "fan-in" you can have a priority or round robin or .. IMO both have their place and we'll likely allow both architectures, but we'll see. I'm flexible but no promises ATM!

I've desgined similar systems and they all (multiple SPSC, single MPSC, hybrids ...) have there place. With a single shared queue is "fair FIFO" order with "fan-in" you can have a priority or round robin or .. IMO they all have a place and we'll likely provide 2 choices, but we'll see. I'm flexible but no promises ATM!

I'll write the design section accordingly: fan-in as a first-class future composition (no commitment), and both in the bench matrix since that's cheap truth either way.

## Preparation

The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):

- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
  - A **plan ladder**.
- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
  cycle's anticipated close-out title.

## Work-N

The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
implement the change. As many as needed:

- Each commit runs through the
  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
  visibility).
- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
  [Pushing](#pushing)).
- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
  scope grows enough (see
  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).

## Close-out

The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
what happens post-squash:

- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
  placeholder opened during Preparation):
  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
    chores `##` header already carries that string).
  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
    back-reference.
  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
  - Replace `## In Progress` with
    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).

Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
see [Pushing](#pushing).

## Numbering

Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
```

317	  included (its message count is reduced under `cfg(miri)`;

329	## Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues
330	
331	Design for the layer above the ring. The pool half is
332	as-built as of the 0.6.0 cycle (`src/pool.rs`: layout,
333	init/attach, alloc/free); descriptor queues and provenance
334	remain design. The sections above are the as-built record of
335	the ring itself.
336	
337	The ring's reserve (`reserve_slot_with`) fuses three acts that
338	a messaging system needs separated: it allocates message
339	memory (the slot),
340	assigns queue position (the reservation is the ring head), and
341	opens a publication window (the guard exclusively borrows the
342	endpoint, blocking all other sends). Getting a message must not
343	imply sending it now — or ever. The fix is a layer, not a ring
344	rework: pools own message memory, queues carry small
345	descriptors, and the existing ring is the unchanged primitive
346	underneath (reserve-to-commit becomes a momentary act, where
347	the fusion is harmless).

  "new_string": "- [loom](https://docs.rs/loom) for exhaustive ordering\n  exploration is a possible follow-on (Ideas).\n\n## MPSC ring (sibling primitive)\n\nDesign for the multi-producer single-consumer ring — a\nsibling type next to the SPSC ring, not a mode of it. The\nSPSC hot path is one Relaxed load + one Acquire load + one\nRelease store per side; any unified type would put at least a\nper-slot sequence check into that path. So the primitives\nstay separate, existing SPSC users pay nothing, and iiac-perf\nmeasures the two directly against each other. Anticipated as\na one-bullet placeholder in\n[Pool topology and phasing](#pool-topology-and-phasing); this\nsection is the full design.\n\n### MPSC protocol\n\nVyukov's bounded MPMC queue restricted to many-producer /\none-consumer: producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`; a per-slot\nsequence word publishes each slot independently. The seq\nexists because claim order and commit order differ under\nconcurrency — the consumer must observe commits, not claims.\n\n- **Sequence array** — `M × AtomicU32`, `seq[i]` initialized\n  to `i`. Slot at free-running position `pos` (masked at\n  access, as SPSC) is:\n  - claimable when `seq == pos`,\n  - committed when `seq == pos + 1`,\n  - released by the consumer storing `seq = pos + M`.\n- **Producer claim** — load `producer_idx` (Relaxed), load\n  that slot's seq (Acquire), branch on the wrapping-signed\n  diff `seq.wrapping_sub(pos) as i32`:\n  - `== 0` — `compare_exchange_weak(pos, pos + 1)` on\n    `producer_idx`; success claims the slot exclusively:\n    write the payload, then `seq.store(pos + 1, Release)`.\n  - `< 0` — full (the slot's previous occupant is not yet\n    released); the `on_full` policy decides retry/give-up.\n  - `> 0` — lost the race to another producer; reload and\n    retry. Not a policy call: the system made progress,\n    only this producer fell behind.\n- **Consumer** — single, as in SPSC, so its side needs no\n  CAS: wait for `seq == c + 1` (Acquire), read, store\n  `seq = c + M` (Release), advance the private index.\n- **Producers never read `consumer_idx`** — fullness falls\n  out of the slot seq, so the header's consumer index line\n  is diagnostic occupancy only.\n- **Free-running u32 + power-of-two M** carry over\n  unchanged: `2^32 % M == 0` keeps seq arithmetic and masked\n  positions wrap-safe.\n- **Atomic floor** — the claim CAS raises the floor for MPSC\n  users only: the module is gated on\n  `target_has_atomic = \"32\"`, and the SPSC ring stays\n  load/store-only (thumbv6m keeps working).\n\n### MPSC API: closure send\n\nThe producer-side guard is replaced by a closure, because in\nMPSC an abandoned *claim* wedges the queue — the consumer\nwaits at that position forever — where SPSC's dropped guard\nwas free (`producer_idx` never moved). Abandonment is made\nunrepresentable rather than handled:\n\n- `MpscRing::init(region, slot_size, capacity)` /\n  `unsafe attach` — mirror the SPSC constructors; own magic\n  and layout_version, so cross-attaching a region of the\n  wrong kind fails toward `BadMagic`.\n- `split() -> (MpscProducer, MpscConsumer)` with\n  `MpscProducer: Clone + Send` — one handle per producing\n  thread; cross-process producers attach. (The endpoint\n  claims word models role slots, not a single producer bit,\n  so N producer claims fit — already noted in its todo.)\n- `producer.send_with::<T>(on_full, fill)` →\n  `Result<(), Full>`, where `fill: impl FnOnce(&mut T)`\n  writes the payload in place — claim, fill, commit on\n  closure return. Commit-by-construction; no public\n  abandonment state. `on_full(attempt) -> bool` is the same\n  wait-policy seam as SPSC (`|_| false` = single probe).\n- `consumer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_empty)` →\n  `ReadSlot` — the SPSC consumer guard survives: consumer\n  abandonment is harmless (drop without release re-delivers\n  the same slot), so the guard shape stays.\n- **Unwind path** — a panic in `fill` runs an internal\n  guard's Drop, which tombstones the claimed slot: a marked\n  commit the consumer releases without delivering. Sketch: a\n  reserved seq bit masked out of the index arithmetic\n  (constrains `M <= 2^30`); encoding is an open question\n  below. Only unwinding reaches it; panic=abort targets\n  never do.\n\n### Overflow readiness\n\nThe sender-private overflow FIFO\n([Overflow FIFO (future)](#overflow-fifo-future)) must\ncompose with MPSC when it lands. Two properties are named\nrequirements here so a later protocol tweak cannot silently\nbreak them:\n\n- **`Full` is a zero-footprint failure** — a send returning\n  `Full` leaves no claim, no tombstone, no protocol residue.\n  The protocol gives this by construction (fullness is\n  observed in the slot seq *before* any CAS; a lost CAS is\n  also stateless), but it is a requirement, not an accident.\n- **`Full` is the overflow seam** — the future MPSC\n  DescSender probes with `|_| false` and on `Err(Full)`\n  appends the buffer to its own pending list. Pending lists\n  stay per-sender (no shared list, no CAS), which preserves\n  exactly the per-producer FIFO order MPSC promises anyway —\n  inter-producer order was never promised.\n\n### MPSC trust\n\nSame posture as the SPSC ring: every shared word is\nuntrusted, corruption degrades service and never causes UB.\n\n- The seq array joins the untrusted set: positions are\n  always masked, so scribbled seqs yield spurious\n  Full/Empty or garbage-but-valid `T`s (zerocopy guarantees\n  representation, not sense) — never out-of-bounds access.\n- A hostile *producer* peer is new surface relative to\n  SPSC: it can claim and never commit (wedging the queue —\n  a liveness loss) or commit garbage. Denial of service\n  stays possible, as it always was; memory safety does not\n  depend on peers.\n\n### Fan-in (composition, not a mode)\n\nThe other standard many-to-one shape: one SPSC ring per\nproducer, the consumer polls N endpoints. Both have a place:\n\n- the shared MPSC ring gives arrival-order fairness (\"fair\n  FIFO\") and one place to poll;\n- fan-in stays load/store-only, has zero producer-to-\n  producer interference, and the consumer chooses the\n  service policy (priority, round-robin, weighted, …).\n\nFan-in is buildable today from shipped SPSC parts; likely\nboth shapes get offered eventually (a fan-in helper as a\nlater convenience — no commitment yet). The bench matrix\nmeasures both from the start.\n\n### MPSC measurement plan\n\niiac-perf (sibling repo) grows an mpsc set alongside `zcr`:\n\n- `zcr-mpsc-1t` — same-thread round-trip: pure protocol\n  overhead (CAS + seq vs load/store), against\n  `zcr-with-1t`.\n- `zcr-mpsc-2t` — 1 producer / 1 consumer cross-core: what\n  MPSC costs when you don't need it, against `zcr-with-2t`.\n- `zcr-mpsc-3t` / `-5t` — 2 / 4 producers, one consumer:\n  claim-contention scaling, the numbers SPSC cannot\n  produce.\n- Fan-in comparator at the same producer counts (N SPSC\n  rings, round-robin poll).\n- We think the shared ring loses to fan-in on raw\n  throughput under producer contention (CAS retries vs\n  disjoint cache lines) and wins on consumer-side latency\n  and fairness at larger N — the matrix exists to check\n  that, not assume it.\n\n### MPSC open questions\n\n- **Seq array padding** — packed `M × 4` bytes (Vyukov's\n  shape; adjacent-slot false sharing) vs line-padded\n  (`M × 64` bytes of overhead). Start packed; the bench\n  matrix decides whether a padded variant earns a layout\n  flag.\n- **Tombstone encoding** — reserved seq bit (masked\n  arithmetic, `M <= 2^30`) vs a per-slot side word. Only\n  the unwind path needs it; the simplest correct encoding\n  wins.\n- **Header consumer index** — keep as diagnostic occupancy\n  (Relaxed stores by the consumer) or drop the line; the\n  `occupancy()` polish todo argues for keeping it.\n\n## Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues"
}

Not a policy call: the system made progress,\n    only this producer fell behind.\n- **Consumer** — single, as in SPSC, so its side needs no\n  CAS: wait for `seq == c + 1` (Acquire), read, store\n  `seq = c + M` (Release), advance the private index.\n- **Producers never read `consumer_idx`** — fullness falls\n  out of the slot seq, so the header's consumer index line\n  is diagnostic occupancy only.\n- **Free-running u32 + power-of-two M** carry over\n  unchanged: `2^32 % M == 0` keeps seq arithmetic and masked\n  positions wrap-safe.\n- **Atomic floor** — the claim CAS raises the floor for MPSC\n  users only: the module is gated on\n  `target_has_atomic = \"32\"`, and the SPSC ring stays\n  load/store-only (thumbv6m keeps working).",
  "new_string": "- **Producer claim** — load `producer_idx` (Relaxed), load\n  that slot's seq (Acquire), branch on the wrapping-signed\n  diff `seq.wrapping_sub(pos) as i32`:\n  - `== 0` — claimable:\n    - `compare_exchange_weak(pos, pos + 1)` on\n      `producer_idx`; success claims the slot exclusively.\n    - Write the payload in place.\n    - `seq.store(pos + 1, Release)` commits.\n  - `< 0` — full:\n    - The slot's previous occupant is not yet released.\n    - The `on_full` policy decides retry vs give-up.\n  - `> 0` — lost the race to another producer:\n    - Reload `producer_idx` and retry.\n    - Not a policy call: the system made progress, only\n      this producer fell behind.\n- **Consumer** — single, as in SPSC, so its side needs no\n  CAS:\n  - wait for `seq == c + 1` (Acquire),\n  - read the slot,\n  - store `seq = c + M` (Release),\n  - advance the private index.\n- **Producers never read `consumer_idx`**:\n  - fullness falls out of the slot seq;\n  - the header's consumer index line is diagnostic\n    occupancy only.\n- **Free-running u32 + power-of-two M** carry over\n  unchanged: `2^32 % M == 0` keeps seq arithmetic and masked\n  positions wrap-safe.\n- **Atomic floor** — the claim CAS raises the floor for MPSC\n  users only:\n  - the module is gated on `target_has_atomic = \"32\"`;\n  - the SPSC ring stays load/store-only (thumbv6m keeps\n    working)."

  "old_string": "The sender-private overflow FIFO\n([Overflow FIFO (future)](#overflow-fifo-future)) must\ncompose with MPSC when it lands. Two properties are named\nrequirements here so a later protocol tweak cannot silently\nbreak them:\n\n- **`Full` is a zero-footprint failure** — a send returning\n  `Full` leaves no claim, no tombstone, no protocol residue.\n  The protocol gives this by construction (fullness is\n  observed in the slot seq *before* any CAS; a lost CAS is\n  also stateless), but it is a requirement, not an accident.\n- **`Full` is the overflow seam** — the future MPSC\n  DescSender probes with `|_| false` and on `Err(Full)`\n  appends the buffer to its own pending list. Pending lists\n  stay per-sender (no shared list, no CAS), which preserves\n  exactly the per-producer FIFO order MPSC promises anyway —\n  inter-producer order was never promised.",
  "new_string": "The sender-private overflow FIFO\n([Overflow FIFO (future)](#overflow-fifo-future)) must\ncompose with MPSC when it lands. Two properties keep that\ntrue; both are named requirements so a later protocol tweak\ncannot silently break them:\n\n- **`Full` is a zero-footprint failure**:\n  - a send returning `Full` leaves no claim, no tombstone,\n    no protocol residue;\n  - the protocol gives this by construction — fullness is\n    observed in the slot seq *before* any CAS, and a lost\n    CAS is also stateless — but it is a requirement, not\n    an accident.\n- **`Full` is the overflow seam**:\n  - the future MPSC DescSender probes with `|_| false` and\n    on `Err(Full)` appends the buffer to its own pending\n    list;\n  - pending lists stay per-sender — no shared list, no\n    CAS;\n  - per-sender draining preserves exactly the per-producer\n    FIFO order MPSC promises anyway — inter-producer order\n    was never promised."
}
```

Only\n  the unwind path needs it; the simplest correct encoding\n  wins.\n- **Header consumer index** — keep as diagnostic occupancy\n  (Relaxed stores by the consumer) or drop the line; the\n  `occupancy()` polish todo argues for keeping it.",

}

1	# Ring buffer design
2	
3	Design notes for the zero-copy ring buffer. This file uses
4	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It covers requirements,
5	constraints, the memory layout, the API, and the validation
6	ladder, and is kept in sync with the implementation in
7	`src/` (as-built): `lib.rs` holds the region-level machinery
8	(header, init/attach/split, geometry helpers) and re-exports
9	the endpoint modules `producer.rs` (Producer, WriteSlot,
10	Full) and `consumer.rs` (Consumer, ReadSlot, Empty).
11	
12	## Goal
13	
14	A ring buffer for passing typed messages between a producer and
15	a consumer with no copying at the boundary: the producer writes
16	its message directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads it
17	in place. The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and
18	inter-process (shared memory).
19	
20	## Requirements
21	
22	- **no_std** — the crate builds with `#![no_std]` and no
23	  `alloc`: fixed capacity, operating over a caller-provided
24	  memory region. Suitable for embedded and kernel-adjacent use.
25	- **Zero-copy via zerocopy** — messages are structs implementing
26	  the [zerocopy](https://docs.rs/zerocopy) traits
27	  (`IntoBytes`, `FromBytes`, `KnownLayout`, `Immutable`); the
28	  buffer transmutes slots into and out of `&T`/`&mut T` with no
29	  serialization step and no `unsafe` at the call site.
30	- **IPC-suitable layout** — the control block (the
31	  producer/consumer indices) and the slot array live in one
32	  contiguous region that
33	  can be mapped into multiple address spaces:
34	  - `#[repr(C)]` explicit layout, stable across processes
35	    compiled from the same source.
36	  - No pointers inside the region — offsets and indices only.
37	- **SPSC first** — single producer, single consumer, lock-free
38	  via atomic producer/consumer indices. MPMC is out of scope
39	  for this cycle;
40	  the layout should not preclude adding it later.
41	- **In-place access API** — `reserve_slot_with` on either
42	  endpoint, then commit (producer) or release (consumer),
43	  exposing references into the buffer rather than returning
44	  values by move.
45	- **Safety** — all `unsafe` encapsulated inside the crate with
46	  documented invariants. The message path (reserve_slot_with,
47	  then commit / release) is fully safe; the one `unsafe` public
48	  entry
49	  is `Ring::attach` — see [API](#api) for why it cannot be
50	  made safe.
51	
52	## Constraints
53	
54	- **Slot geometry: M slots × N bytes** — a *slot* is one
55	  fixed-size buffer in the ring; the ring holds M of them.
56	  - M (capacity) is a power of two — index wrapping is a
57	    mask, no division; checked at construction.
58	  - N (slot size) is a multiple of the cache-line size, and
59	    each slot is cache-line aligned — adjacent slots never
60	    share a line, and any `T` with `align_of::<T>()` up to
61	    the line size fits without padding logic.
62	  - This trades space for isolation (a 16-byte message
63	    occupies a full 64-byte slot) — the right default for
64	    IPC; a packed-slot variant is a possible follow-on.
65	- **Monotonic indices** — producer/consumer indices are
66	  free-running unsigned counters (wrap via masking), so
67	  full/empty are distinguishable without a separate flag or
68	  a sacrificial slot.
69	- **Cache-line separation** — producer- and consumer-owned
70	  indices are padded/aligned to separate cache lines to avoid
71	  false sharing.
72	- **Typed access over untyped slots** — the slot geometry
73	  (M, N) is a property of the buffer, independent of any
74	  message type; a `T` is a zerocopy view into a slot, valid
75	  when `size_of::<T>() <= N` and `align_of::<T>()` divides
76	  the slot alignment (checked at construction or compile
77	  time). Variable-length framed messages are a possible
78	  follow-on, not in this cycle.
79	- **zerocopy, core-only** — depend on zerocopy with default
80	  features compatible with `no_std`; no other runtime
81	  dependencies.
82	
83	## Memory layout
84	
85	One contiguous `#[repr(C)]` region, mappable into multiple
```

The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and\ninter-process (shared memory).\n\n## Terminology\n\nThe vocabulary the rest of the document builds on, defined\nonce before first use:\n\n- **slot** — a cache-line-aligned `[u8; N]`: one of the\n  ring's M fixed-size buffers. Untyped storage — a message\n  is a zerocopy `&T` / `&mut T` view into it (geometry\n  details in [Constraints](#constraints)).\n- **reserve** — take exclusive access to a slot through a\n  guard:\n  - the producer reserves the next free slot\n    (`WriteSlot`);\n  - the consumer reserves the oldest unread slot\n    (`ReadSlot`).\n- **commit** — the producer publishes its slot to the\n  consumer; the handoff of ownership away from the writer:\n  - SPSC: the `producer_idx` Release store;\n  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store.\n- **release** — the consumer hands its slot back for\n  reuse; the matching handoff in the other direction:\n  - SPSC: the `consumer_idx` Release store;\n  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store (`pos + M`).\n- **claim** — MPSC only: a producer wins exclusive\n  ownership of a slot position by CAS on the shared\n  producer index; writing and committing follow. Claim\n  order and commit order can differ — see\n  [MPSC ring (sibling primitive)](#mpsc-ring-sibling-primitive).\n\n## Requirements"
}

1	# Ring buffer design
2	
3	Design notes for the zero-copy ring buffer. This file uses
4	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It covers terminology,
5	requirements, constraints, the memory layout, the API, and
6	the validation ladder, and is kept in sync with the
7	implementation in
8	`src/` (as-built): `lib.rs` holds the region-level machinery
9	(header, init/attach/split, geometry helpers) and re-exports
10	the endpoint modules `producer.rs` (Producer, WriteSlot,
11	Full) and `consumer.rs` (Consumer, ReadSlot, Empty).
12	
13	## Goal
14	
15	A ring buffer for passing typed messages between a producer and
16	a consumer with no copying at the boundary: the producer writes
17	its message directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads it
18	in place. The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and
19	inter-process (shared memory).
20	
21	## Terminology
22	
23	The vocabulary the rest of the document builds on, defined
24	once before first use:
25	
26	- **slot** — a cache-line-aligned `[u8; N]`: one of the
27	  ring's M fixed-size buffers. Untyped storage — a message
28	  is a zerocopy `&T` / `&mut T` view into it (geometry
29	  details in [Constraints](#constraints)).
30	- **reserve** — take exclusive access to a slot through a
31	  guard:
32	  - the producer reserves the next free slot
33	    (`WriteSlot`);
34	  - the consumer reserves the oldest unread slot
35	    (`ReadSlot`).
36	- **commit** — the producer publishes its slot to the
37	  consumer; the handoff of ownership away from the writer:
38	  - SPSC: the `producer_idx` Release store;
39	  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store.
40	- **release** — the consumer hands its slot back for
41	  reuse; the matching handoff in the other direction:
42	  - SPSC: the `consumer_idx` Release store;
43	  - MPSC: the slot's seq Release store (`pos + M`).
44	- **claim** — MPSC only: a producer wins exclusive
45	  ownership of a slot position by CAS on the shared
46	  producer index; writing and committing follow. Claim
47	  order and commit order can differ — see
48	  [MPSC ring (sibling primitive)](#mpsc-ring-sibling-primitive).
49	
50	## Requirements
51	
52	- **no_std** — the crate builds with `#![no_std]` and no
53	  `alloc`: fixed capacity, operating over a caller-provided
54	  memory region. Suitable for embedded and kernel-adjacent use.
55	- **Zero-copy via zerocopy** — messages are structs implementing

> AGENT

Done. The doc now has a `## Terminology` section between Goal and Requirements (lines 21–48 above) defining the five lifecycle terms before any use:

- **slot** — "a cache-line-aligned `[u8; N]`" per your Rusty phrasing, with the zerocopy-view note and a pointer to Constraints for the M×N geometry details.
- **reserve** — guard-based exclusive access, producer/consumer sub-bullets.
- **commit** / **release** — the two ownership handoffs, each with SPSC/MPSC mechanism sub-bullets (they differ: index store vs seq store).
- **claim** — flagged MPSC-only, with the claim-order ≠ commit-order point and a link into the MPSC section.

The Constraints bullet no longer re-defines the term — it now reads "the ring holds M [slots](#terminology) of N bytes each."

One term collision I noticed while doing this: the **endpoint claims word** (role claims — claiming producer/consumer *roles*) uses "claim" in a different sense than the MPSC slot-position claim. The Terminology entry says "MPSC only ... slot position," which implicitly disambiguates, but I can add an explicit "not to be confused with the endpoint claims word (role claims)" note if you'd like.

Keep reviewing — I'll hold for your go-ahead on the push.

1	# Chores-01
2	
3	Chores-XX files use [Prose form](../../AGENTS.md#prose-form). They
4	contain discussions and notes on various chores in github compatible
5	markdown. There is also a [todo.md](../todo.md) file that tracks
6	tasks and in general there should be a chore section for each task
7	with the why and how this task will be completed.
8	
9	## docs: completed dummy chore (0.1.0)
10	
11	A completed dummy chore description.
12	
13	## chore: dummy chore (TBD)
14	
15	A dummy chore description.
16	
17	## docs: versioning SSOT + generic conventions
18	
19	Commits:
20	
21	Adopt the converged generic convention set from fc, so the
22	template's `AGENTS.md`, `cycle-protocol.md`, and `versioning.md`
23	are byte-identical to fc's — future repos copy them verbatim and
24	edit nothing. The template gains the versioning SSOT and the
