Consumer keeps the SPSC guard shape (drop\nre-delivers) and stays CAS-free.\n\n- src/mpsc.rs -> src/mpsc/mod.rs: module split; adds\n  TOMBSTONE, split() -> (MpscProducer, MpscConsumer), and the\n  protocol tests: roundtrip/full/empty, policy counting with\n  zero-footprint Full, read-guard abandon, tombstone skip +\n  slot reuse, u32-wrap with reseeded seqs, threaded 2-producer\n  stress (per-producer FIFO asserted) via Clone and via &self\n  sharing (Sync).\n- src/mpsc/producer.rs: MpscProducer (Clone + Send + Sync,\n  &self send_with) — Vyukov claim loop (lost race reloads\n  without a policy call; stale-index recheck before on_full so\n  a raced tombstone is not a spurious Full),\n  TombstoneOnUnwind drop guard disarmed by mem::forget on the\n  normal path.\n- src/mpsc/consumer.rs: MpscConsumer + MpscReadSlot —\n  seq-Acquire wait, tombstone skip inline (progress, not an\n  attempt), release stores consumer_idx (Relaxed, private\n  resume state) then seq = pos+capacity (Release, the\n  protocol handoff).\n- src/lib.rs: re-export the endpoint types.\n- notes/todo.md: ladder markers advanced.\n- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: version-of-record 0.11.0-2.\n\nValidation: fmt/clippy clean, 40 tests pass, full Miri suite\nclean (threaded + unwind paths included).\nEOF\n)\" \\\n  --yes 2>&1 | tail -4",

1	//! Demo binary: both primitives working across threads,
2	//! with throughput printed — run `cargo run --release`, or
3	//! `cargo install --path . --locked` and run
4	//! `zc-ring-x1-demo`. `-V`/`--version` prints the
5	//! version-of-record so you know exactly which build you
6	//! are testing.
7	//!
8	//! - Part 1, the ring: an SPSC pair moves typed messages
9	//!   in place (reserve → write → commit; reserve → read →
10	//!   release) — first both ends on one thread
11	//!   (the ring's own cost), then one producer thread to
12	//!   one consumer thread: unpinned, pinned to two different
13	//!   physical cores, and pinned to one physical core's two
14	//!   SMT siblings (shared L1/L2, when the CPU has SMT).
15	//! - Part 2, the pool: an allocator thread allocs and
16	//!   fills `BufSlot`s and hands them to a freer thread —
17	//!   "send" today is moving the guard (see the README's
18	//!   usage model); getting a buffer implies nothing about
19	//!   when it is sent or freed.
20	//! - The composed form — descriptors through the ring,
21	//!   payloads at rest in pool buffers — runs between them:
22	//!   alloc → into_desc → ring → resolve → free, with the
23	//!   same placement ladder as the raw ring.
24	
25	use std::time::Instant;
26	
27	use zc_ring_x1::{
28	    BufSlot, CACHE_LINE_SIZE, Desc, Empty, Exhausted, Full, Pool, PoolRegistry, Ring, policy,
29	};
30	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
31	
32	/// Messages moved per part.
33	const COUNT: u64 = 1_000_000;
34	
35	/// Ring slots / pool buffers (small on purpose: recycling
36	/// under pressure is the interesting case).
37	const DEPTH: u32 = 64;
38	
39	/// The demo message; one word carrying the sequence number
40	/// the consumer asserts.
41	#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
42	#[repr(C)]
43	struct Msg {
44	    seq: u64,
45	}
46	
47	/// Region for either primitive: biggest header (ring, 4
48	/// lines) + DEPTH one-line slots/buffers.
49	#[repr(C, align(64))]
50	struct Region([u8; 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE + DEPTH as usize * CACHE_LINE_SIZE]);
51	
52	/// Group a count into comma-separated thousands
53	/// (`1234567` → `"1,234,567"`).
54	fn commas(n: u64) -> String {
55	    let digits = n.to_string();
56	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(digits.len() + digits.len() / 3);
57	    for (i, c) in digits.chars().enumerate() {
58	        if i > 0 && (digits.len() - i).is_multiple_of(3) {
59	            out.push(',');
60	        }
61	        out.push(c);
62	    }
63	    out
64	}
65	
66	/// Print one part's line: msgs/sec (comma-grouped) and
67	/// ns/msg from the part's elapsed seconds.
68	fn report(label: &str, secs: f64) {
69	    let rate = commas((COUNT as f64 / secs) as u64);
70	    let ns_per_msg = secs * 1e9 / COUNT as f64;
71	    println!("{label:<46} {rate:>12} msgs/sec  {ns_per_msg:>7.1} ns/msg");
72	}
73	
74	/// Parse a /sys cpu-list string ("0,12" or "0-2,6") into cpu
75	/// numbers; malformed pieces are skipped.
76	#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
77	fn parse_cpu_list(s: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
78	    let mut out = Vec::new();
79	    for part in s.trim().split(',') {
80	        match part.split_once('-') {
81	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
82	                if let (Ok(lo), Ok(hi)) = (lo.parse::<usize>(), hi.parse::<usize>()) {
83	                    out.extend(lo..=hi);
84	                }
85	            }
86	            None => {
87	                if let Ok(n) = part.parse() {
88	                    out.push(n);
89	                }
90	            }
91	        }
92	    }
93	    out
94	}
95	
96	/// A `(producer cpu, consumer cpu)` pin for a 2t run;
97	/// `None` means the pair is unavailable / leave unpinned.
98	type PinPair = Option<(usize, usize)>;
99	
100	/// Discover two cpu pairs for the pinned 2t runs from
101	/// /sys/devices/system/cpu; `None` when the machine lacks the
102	/// shape (or not Linux).
103	///
104	/// - `.0` — same physical core: cpu0 and its SMT sibling
105	///   (shared L1/L2, the cheapest handoff).
106	/// - `.1` — different physical cores: cpu0 and a core outside
107	///   cpu0's L3 group when one exists (the farthest handoff),
108	///   else any non-sibling core.
109	#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
110	fn discover_pin_pairs() -> (PinPair, PinPair) {
111	    let read = |path: &str| std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok();
112	    let siblings = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list")
113	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
114	        .unwrap_or_default();
115	    let smt = (siblings.len() >= 2).then(|| (siblings[0], siblings[1]));
116	    let online = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/online")
117	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
118	        .unwrap_or_default();
119	    let l3 = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/cache/index3/shared_cpu_list")
120	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
121	        .unwrap_or_else(|| siblings.clone());
122	    let far = online
123	        .iter()
124	        .copied()
125	        .find(|c| !l3.contains(c))
126	        .or_else(|| {
127	            online
128	                .iter()
129	                .copied()
130	                .find(|c| *c != 0 && !siblings.contains(c))
131	        });
132	    (smt, far.map(|c| (0, c)))
133	}
134	
135	/// Non-Linux stub: no pinned runs.
136	#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
137	fn discover_pin_pairs() -> (PinPair, PinPair) {
138	    (None, None)
139	}
140	
141	/// Pin the calling thread to `cpu` via sched_setaffinity;
142	/// panics on failure (a demo run with a silently ignored pin
143	/// would report a mislabeled number).
144	#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
145	fn pin_to_cpu(cpu: usize) {
146	    // SAFETY: cpu_set_t is a plain bitmask; CPU_ZERO/CPU_SET
147	    // initialize it fully before sched_setaffinity reads it.
148	    unsafe {
149	        let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = std::mem::zeroed();
150	        libc::CPU_ZERO(&mut set);
151	        libc::CPU_SET(cpu, &mut set);
152	        let rc = libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &set);
153	        assert_eq!(rc, 0, "sched_setaffinity({cpu}) failed");
154	    }
155	}
156	
157	/// Non-Linux stub: pinning is a no-op, so only the 1t runs
158	/// reach it (discover_pin_pairs returns no pairs); present
159	/// so the demo compiles everywhere.
160	#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
161	fn pin_to_cpu(_cpu: usize) {}
162	
163	/// Move COUNT messages single thread through the ring,
164	/// pinned to cpu 0 for consistency with the pinned 2t runs;
165	/// return elapsed seconds.
166	///
167	/// The loop runs in a scoped thread rather than pinning the
168	/// main thread: spawned threads inherit the main thread's
169	/// affinity mask, which would squeeze every later part onto
170	/// cpu 0.
171	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t() -> f64 {
172	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
173	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
174	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
175	        .split();
176	
177	    let start = Instant::now();
178	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
179	        s.spawn(move || {
180	            pin_to_cpu(0);
181	            for i in 0..COUNT {
182	                match producer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {
183	                    Ok(mut slot) => {
184	                        slot.seq = i;
185	                        slot.commit();
186	                    }
187	                    Err(Full) => {
188	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
189	                    }
190	                }
191	                match consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {
192	                    Ok(msg) => {
193	                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
194	                        msg.release();
195	                    }
196	                    Err(Empty) => {
197	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
198	                    }
199	                }
200	            }
201	        });
202	    });
203	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
204	}
205	
206	/// Move COUNT messages producer-thread → consumer-thread
207	/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.
208	///
209	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
210	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
211	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
212	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
213	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
214	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
215	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
216	        .split();
217	
218	    let start = Instant::now();
219	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
220	        s.spawn(move || {
221	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
222	                pin_to_cpu(p);
223	            }
224	            for i in 0..COUNT {
225	                let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
226	                slot.seq = i;
227	                slot.commit();
228	            }
229	        });
230	        s.spawn(move || {
231	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
232	                pin_to_cpu(c);
233	            }
234	            for i in 0..COUNT {
235	                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
236	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
237	                msg.release();
238	            }
239	        });
240	    });
241	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
242	}
243	
244	/// The composed flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: one pool
245	/// message allocated outside the timed loop; each iteration
246	/// populates it, converts guard → descriptor, rings the
247	/// descriptor across, and resolves the guard back; return
248	/// elapsed seconds.
249	///
250	/// - Isolates messaging cost (into_desc + ring + resolve)
251	///   from the pool cycle — pool_alloc_free_1t reports that
252	///   separately.
253	/// - The guard and the descriptor are the two exclusive
254	///   forms of ownership, so the per-iteration conversion
255	///   cannot hoist: converting *is* the send-side handoff.
256	///   The 2t variant keeps alloc/free per message because
257	///   there ownership genuinely leaves the producer.
258	fn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t() -> f64 {
259	    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
260	    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
261	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
262	    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();
263	    let pool_id = registry.register(pool.resolver()).unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room
264	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
265	        Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
266	            .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
267	            .split();
268	
269	    let start = Instant::now();
270	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
271	        s.spawn(move || {
272	            pin_to_cpu(0);
273	            let mut buf_slot = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // OK: fresh pool, DEPTH buffers free
274	            for i in 0..COUNT {
275	                buf_slot.seq = i;
276	                let desc = registry
277	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf_slot)
278	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
279	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
280	                match producer.reserve_slot_with::<Desc>(|_| false) {
281	                    Ok(mut slot) => {
282	                        *slot = desc;
283	                        slot.commit();
284	                    }
285	                    Err(Full) => {
286	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
287	                    }
288	                }
289	                buf_slot = match consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Desc>(|_| false) {
290	                    Ok(slot) => {
291	                        let desc = *slot;
292	                        slot.release();
293	                        // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into
294	                        // the ring by into_desc above and is
295	                        // resolved exactly once, same thread.
296	                        let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: desc came from into_desc on this pool
297	                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
298	                        msg
299	                    }
300	                    Err(Empty) => {
301	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
302	                    }
303	                };
304	            }
305	            buf_slot.free();
306	        });
307	    });
308	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
309	}
310	
311	/// The composed flow producer-thread → consumer-thread:
312	/// alloc + fill pool messages on the producer, descriptors
313	/// cross the SPSC ring, the consumer resolves and frees;
314	/// return elapsed seconds.
315	///
316	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
317	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
318	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
319	fn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
320	    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
321	    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
322	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
323	    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();
324	    let pool_id = registry.register(pool.resolver()).unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room
325	    let registry = &registry;
326	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
327	        Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
328	            .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
329	            .split();
330	
331	    let start = Instant::now();
332	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
333	        s.spawn(move || {
334	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
335	                pin_to_cpu(p);
336	            }
337	            for i in 0..COUNT {
338	                let mut buf_slot = loop {
339	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
340	                        Ok(buf_slot) => break buf_slot,
341	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
342	                    }
343	                };
344	                buf_slot.seq = i;
345	                let desc = registry
346	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf_slot)
347	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
348	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
349	                let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_with::<Desc>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
350	                *slot = desc;
351	                slot.commit();
352	            }
353	        });
354	        s.spawn(move || {
355	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
356	                pin_to_cpu(c);
357	            }
358	            for i in 0..COUNT {
359	                let desc = {
360	                    let slot = consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Desc>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
361	                    let desc = *slot;
362	                    slot.release();
363	                    desc
364	                };
365	                // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into the ring
366	                // by the producer and read after the commit →
367	                // reserve handoff (happens-before); each is
368	                // resolved exactly once.
369	                let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: descs here only come from the producer's into_desc
370	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
371	                msg.free();
372	            }
373	        });
374	    });
375	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
376	}
377	
378	/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,
379	/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std
380	/// channel); return elapsed seconds.
381	///
382	/// - `pin` — `Some((a, f))` pins the allocator to cpu `a`
383	///   and the freer to cpu `f`; `None` lets the scheduler
384	///   place them (the number then depends on where they
385	///   land).
386	fn std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
387	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
388	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
389	
390	    let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel::<BufSlot<'_, Msg>>(DEPTH as usize);
391	    let start = Instant::now();
392	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
393	        s.spawn(move || {
394	            if let Some((a, _)) = pin {
395	                pin_to_cpu(a);
396	            }
397	            for i in 0..COUNT {
398	                loop {
399	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
400	                        Ok(mut buf_slot) => {
401	                            buf_slot.seq = i;
402	                            tx.send(buf_slot).unwrap();
403	                            break;
404	                        }
405	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
406	                    }
407	                }
408	            }
409	        });
410	        s.spawn(move || {
411	            if let Some((_, f)) = pin {
412	                pin_to_cpu(f);
413	            }
414	            for i in 0..COUNT {
415	                let buf_slot = rx.recv().unwrap();
416	                assert_eq!(buf_slot.seq, i);
417	                buf_slot.free();
418	            }
419	        });
420	    });
421	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
422	}
423	
424	/// The std-channel flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: alloc
425	/// a pool message, move its guard through a sync_channel,
426	/// receive and free it; return elapsed seconds.
427	fn std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_1t() -> f64 {
428	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
429	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
430	    let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel::<BufSlot<'_, Msg>>(DEPTH as usize);
431	
432	    let start = Instant::now();
433	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
434	        s.spawn(move || {
435	            pin_to_cpu(0);
436	            for i in 0..COUNT {
437	                let mut buf_slot = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // OK: alloc+free per iteration, DEPTH never exceeded
438	                buf_slot.seq = i;
439	                tx.send(buf_slot).unwrap(); // OK: capacity DEPTH, at most one in flight
440	                let buf_slot = rx.recv().unwrap(); // OK: just sent on this thread
441	                assert_eq!(buf_slot.seq, i);
442	                buf_slot.free();
443	            }
444	        });
445	    });
446	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
447	}
448	
449	/// Alloc → write → free COUNT messages on one thread pinned
450	/// to cpu 0 — the pool's own cost, no channel, no second
451	/// thread; return elapsed seconds.
452	///
453	/// Runs in a scoped thread like the other pinned parts, so
454	/// the main thread's affinity stays untouched.
455	fn pool_alloc_free_1t() -> f64 {
456	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
457	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
458	
459	    let start = Instant::now();
460	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
461	        s.spawn(move || {
462	            pin_to_cpu(0);
463	            for i in 0..COUNT {
464	                let mut buf_slot = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // OK: alloc+free per iteration, DEPTH never exceeded
465	                buf_slot.seq = i;
466	                std::hint::black_box(buf_slot.seq);
467	                buf_slot.free();
468	            }
469	        });
470	    });
471	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
472	}
473	
474	/// The same loop through the global allocator (Box::new →
475	/// write → drop) for comparison, pinned to cpu 0; return
476	/// elapsed seconds.
477	///
478	/// Runs in a scoped thread like the other pinned parts, so
479	/// the main thread's affinity stays untouched.
480	fn global_alloc_free_1t() -> f64 {
481	    let start = Instant::now();
482	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
483	        s.spawn(move || {
484	            pin_to_cpu(0);
485	            for i in 0..COUNT {
486	                let mut buf = std::hint::black_box(Box::new(Msg { seq: 0 }));
487	                buf.seq = i;
488	                std::hint::black_box(buf.seq);
489	                drop(buf);
490	            }
491	        });
492	    });
493	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
494	}
495	
496	/// Run both parts and print their throughput; `-V` /
497	/// `--version` prints the version-of-record and exits.
498	fn main() {
499	    let banner = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
500	    println!("{banner}");
501	    if std::env::args().any(|a| a == "-V" || a == "--version") {
502	        return;
503	    }
504	    println!("demo: {} messages each, depth {DEPTH}", commas(COUNT));
505	    let (smt, far) = discover_pin_pairs();
506	
507	    // Alloc/free baselines, then message flows — like
508	    // compares with like within each block.
509	    report("pool_alloc_free_1t (core 0):", pool_alloc_free_1t());
510	    report("global_alloc_free_1t (core 0):", global_alloc_free_1t());
511	
512	    // Single thread, core 0.
513	    println!();
514	    report("spsc_ring_one_msg_1t (core 0):", spsc_ring_one_msg_1t());
515	    report(
516	        "spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t (core 0):",
517	        spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t(),
518	    );
519	    report(
520	        "std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_1t (core 0):",
521	        std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_1t(),
522	    );
523	
524	    // Two threads, scheduler-placed.
525	    println!();
526	    report(
527	        "spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (unpinned):",
528	        spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(None),
529	    );
530	    report(
531	        "spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t (unpinned):",
532	        spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(None),
533	    );
534	    report(
535	        "std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (unpinned):",
536	        std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t(None),
537	    );
538	
539	    // Two threads, different physical cores.
540	    println!();
541	    match far {
542	        Some((p, c)) => {
543	            report(
544	                &format!("spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (diff cores {p}+{c}):"),
545	                spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(far),
546	            );
547	            report(
548	                &format!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t (diff cores {p}+{c}):"),
549	                spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(far),
550	            );
551	            report(
552	                &format!("std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (diff cores {p}+{c}):"),
553	                std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t(far),
554	            );
555	        }
556	        None => {
557	            println!("2t diff-cores runs:");
558	            println!("  skipped, only one core found");
559	        }
560	    }
561	
562	    // Two threads, one physical core (SMT siblings).
563	    println!();
564	    match smt {
565	        Some((p, c)) => {
566	            report(
567	                &format!("spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core {p}+{c}):"),
568	                spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(smt),
569	            );
570	            report(
571	                &format!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t (same core {p}+{c}):"),
572	                spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(smt),
573	            );
574	            report(
575	                &format!("std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (same core {p}+{c}):"),
576	                std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t(smt),
577	            );
578	        }
579	        None => {
580	            println!("2t same-core runs:");
581	            println!("  skipped, no SMT sibling found");
582	        }
583	    }
584	}
585	
```

  "new_string": "/// Move COUNT messages single thread through the MPSC ring,\n/// pinned to cpu 0 — the sibling of spsc_ring_one_msg_1t, so\n/// the two lines read as the seam between the protocols\n/// (claim CAS + seq vs load/store); return elapsed seconds.\nfn mpsc_ring_one_msg_1t() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = MpscRegion([0; size_of::<MpscRegion>()]);\n    let (producer, mut consumer) = MpscRing::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: MpscRegion is sized/aligned for header + seqs + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                producer\n                    .send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i)\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: room is guaranteed, the consumer drains in lockstep\n                match consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false) {\n                    Ok(msg) => {\n                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                        msg.release();\n                    }\n                    Err(Empty) => {\n                        panic!(\"mpsc_ring_one_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// Move COUNT messages producer-thread → consumer-thread\n/// through the MPSC ring — the sibling of\n/// spsc_ring_one_msg_2t at the same placements, measuring\n/// what the MPSC protocol costs when you don't need multiple\n/// producers; return elapsed seconds.\n///\n/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and\n///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place\n///   them (the number then depends on where they land).\nfn mpsc_ring_one_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {\n    let mut region = MpscRegion([0; size_of::<MpscRegion>()]);\n    let (producer, mut consumer) = MpscRing::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: MpscRegion is sized/aligned for header + seqs + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(p);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                producer\n                    .send_with::<Msg>(policy::spin, |m| m.seq = i)\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up\n            }\n        });\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(c);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// Move COUNT messages (COUNT/2 per producer) from two\n/// producer threads into one consumer — the line the SPSC\n/// ring cannot produce: claim contention on the shared\n/// producer index. Unpinned (a pinned variant would need a\n/// third discovered cpu); per-producer FIFO is asserted, the\n/// interleave is whatever the claim race said. Returns\n/// elapsed seconds.\nfn mpsc_ring_one_msg_3t() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = MpscRegion([0; size_of::<MpscRegion>()]);\n    let (producer, mut consumer) = MpscRing::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: MpscRegion is sized/aligned for header + seqs + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        for p in 0..2u64 {\n            let producer = producer.clone();\n            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT / 2 {\n                    producer\n                        .send_with::<Msg>(policy::spin, |m| {\n                            m.seq = i;\n                            m.val = p;\n                        })\n                        .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up\n                }\n            });\n        }\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            let mut next = [0u64; 2];\n            for _ in 0..COUNT {\n                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up\n                let p = msg.val as usize;\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, next[p], \"per-producer order broken\");\n                next[p] += 1;\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// The composed flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: one pool\n/// message allocated outside the timed loop; each iteration\n/// populates it, converts guard → descriptor, rings the\n/// descriptor across, and resolves the guard back; return\n/// elapsed seconds."
}

1	//! Single-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, closure
2	//! (`reserve_slot_with`) API tier.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	
6	use zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};
7	
8	use crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, leak_ring};
9	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
10	
11	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
12	pub const NAME: &str = "zcr-with-1t";
13	
14	/// Same-thread round-trip reserving through `reserve_slot_with`
15	/// with an app-supplied spin closure.
16	///
17	/// - The closure never runs here (one message in flight, never
18	///   full/empty), so the measurement is the cost of the `_with`
19	///   wrapper's fast path — a single claim with no contention.
20	pub struct ZcrWith1Thread {
21	    producer: Producer<'static>,
22	    consumer: Consumer<'static>,
23	    counter: u64,
24	}
25	
26	impl ZcrWith1Thread {
27	    /// Construct the bench over one fresh leaked ring.
28	    pub fn new() -> Self {
29	        let (producer, consumer) = leak_ring();
30	        Self {
31	            producer,
32	            consumer,
33	            counter: 0,
34	        }
35	    }
36	}
37	
38	impl Bench for ZcrWith1Thread {
39	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
40	        "zcr-with-1t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_with round-trip (1 thread)"
41	    }
42	
43	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
44	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
45	        let mut slot = self
46	            .producer
47	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
48	                core::hint::spin_loop();
49	                true
50	            })
51	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
52	        *slot = self.counter;
53	        slot.commit();
54	        let slot = self
55	            .consumer
56	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
57	                core::hint::spin_loop();
58	                true
59	            })
60	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
61	        let v = *slot;
62	        slot.release();
63	        black_box(v)
64	    }
65	}
66	
67	/// Registry entry point.
68	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
69	    let mut bench = ZcrWith1Thread::new();
70	    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
71	    harness::print_report(
72	        bench.name(),
73	        outer,
74	        inner,
75	        duration_s,
76	        &hist,
77	        cfg,
78	        suspended_s,
79	    );
80	}
81	
```

1	//! Shared setup for the `zcr-*` benches: leaked ring regions
2	//! and `'static` endpoint construction over the sibling
3	//! `zc-ring-x1` crate.
4	
5	use zc_ring_x1::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, Consumer, Header, Producer, Ring};
6	
7	/// Slot payload for every zcr bench: the round-trip counter.
8	/// `u64` satisfies the zerocopy bounds and matches the message
9	/// shape of the mpsc/ice benches.
10	pub type Msg = u64;
11	
12	/// Shutdown sentinel the 2t benches send instead of a counter
13	/// value; the echo worker exits on receipt without replying.
14	/// The counter increments skip it (see each bench's `step`).
15	pub const STOP: Msg = u64::MAX;
16	
17	/// Slots per ring — a power of two, comfortably above the one
18	/// message ever in flight in the round-trip benches.
19	pub const CAPACITY: u32 = 8;
20	
21	/// Region bytes: the four-cache-line [`Header`] plus
22	/// [`CAPACITY`] slots of one cache line each.
23	const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<Header>() + CACHE_LINE_SIZE * CAPACITY as usize;
24	
25	/// Cache-line-aligned backing region for one ring, matching
26	/// `Ring::init`'s alignment requirement.
27	#[repr(C, align(64))]
28	struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);
29	
30	/// Build a ring over a leaked region and split it into
31	/// `'static` endpoint handles.
32	///
33	/// - Leaked on purpose: the 2t benches move one endpoint into a
34	///   spawned worker thread, so the region must outlive the
35	///   bench struct. ~768 B per ring for the process lifetime is
36	///   fine in a bench binary.
37	pub fn leak_ring() -> (Producer<'static>, Consumer<'static>) {
38	    let region: &'static mut Region = Box::leak(Box::new(Region([0; REGION_BYTES])));
39	    Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, CAPACITY)
40	        .expect("geometry is valid by construction")
41	        .split()
42	}
43	
```

1	//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, closure
2	//! (`reserve_slot_with`) API tier, spin waits.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	use std::thread;
6	
7	use zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};
8	
9	use crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};
10	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
11	use crate::pin;
12	
13	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
14	pub const NAME: &str = "zcr-with-2t";
15	
16	/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings,
17	/// both ends waiting inside `reserve_slot_with` with an
18	/// app-supplied spin closure.
19	///
20	/// - Wait policy: a `spin_loop` hint per failed attempt, so the
21	///   measurement is the `_with` claim under real cross-core
22	///   traffic.
23	/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker
24	///   exits on receipt without replying.
25	pub struct ZcrWith2Thread {
26	    req_tx: Producer<'static>,
27	    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,
28	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
29	    counter: u64,
30	}
31	
32	impl ZcrWith2Thread {
33	    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked
34	    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.
35	    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {
36	        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();
37	        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();
38	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
39	            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);
40	            loop {
41	                let v = {
42	                    let slot = req_rx
43	                        .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
44	                            core::hint::spin_loop();
45	                            true
46	                        })
47	                        .expect("spin closure never gives up");
48	                    let v = *slot;
49	                    slot.release();
50	                    v
51	                };
52	                if v == STOP {
53	                    break;
54	                }
55	                let mut slot = resp_tx
56	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
57	                        core::hint::spin_loop();
58	                        true
59	                    })
60	                    .expect("spin closure never gives up");
61	                *slot = v;
62	                slot.commit();
63	            }
64	        });
65	        Self {
66	            req_tx,
67	            resp_rx,
68	            worker: Some(worker),
69	            counter: 0,
70	        }
71	    }
72	}
73	
74	impl Bench for ZcrWith2Thread {
75	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
76	        "zcr-with-2t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_with round-trip (2 threads, spin)"
77	    }
78	
79	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
80	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
81	        if self.counter == STOP {
82	            self.counter = 1;
83	        }
84	        let mut slot = self
85	            .req_tx
86	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
87	                core::hint::spin_loop();
88	                true
89	            })
90	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
91	        *slot = self.counter;
92	        slot.commit();
93	        let slot = self
94	            .resp_rx
95	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
96	                core::hint::spin_loop();
97	                true
98	            })
99	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
100	        let v = *slot;
101	        slot.release();
102	        black_box(v)
103	    }
104	}
105	
106	impl Drop for ZcrWith2Thread {
107	    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.
108	    fn drop(&mut self) {
109	        let mut slot = self
110	            .req_tx
111	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
112	                core::hint::spin_loop();
113	                true
114	            })
115	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
116	        *slot = STOP;
117	        slot.commit();
118	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
119	            let _ = worker.join();
120	        }
121	    }
122	}
123	
124	/// Registry entry point.
125	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
126	    let mut bench = ZcrWith2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));
127	    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
128	    harness::print_report(
129	        bench.name(),
130	        outer,
131	        inner,
132	        duration_s,
133	        &hist,
134	        cfg,
135	        suspended_s,
136	    );
137	}
138

1	//! Bench registry. Each bench module exposes `NAME` (CLI id) and
2	//! `run` (entry point). Add a bench by creating a module and
3	//! appending it to [`REGISTRY`].
4	
5	pub mod ice_ps_1t;
6	pub mod ice_ps_2t;
7	pub mod ice_rr_1t;
8	pub mod ice_rr_2t;
9	pub mod min_now;
10	pub mod mpsc_1t;
11	pub mod mpsc_2t;
12	pub mod mpsc_2t_spin;
13	pub mod probe_mpsc_2t;
14	pub mod producer_consumer;
15	pub mod std_now;
16	pub mod tp2_pc;
17	pub mod tp_pc;
18	pub mod zcr_common;
19	pub mod zcr_with_1t;
20	pub mod zcr_with_2t;
21	
22	use crate::harness::RunCfg;
23	
24	/// Bench entry-point signature.
25	pub type RunFn = fn(&RunCfg);
26	
27	/// Static list of every registered bench, in display order.
28	pub const REGISTRY: &[(&str, RunFn)] = &[
29	    (min_now::NAME, min_now::run),
30	    (std_now::NAME, std_now::run),
31	    (mpsc_1t::NAME, mpsc_1t::run),
32	    (mpsc_2t::NAME, mpsc_2t::run),
33	    (mpsc_2t_spin::NAME, mpsc_2t_spin::run),
34	    (probe_mpsc_2t::NAME, probe_mpsc_2t::run),
35	    (producer_consumer::NAME, producer_consumer::run),
36	    (tp_pc::NAME, tp_pc::run),
37	    (tp2_pc::NAME, tp2_pc::run),
38	    (ice_ps_1t::NAME, ice_ps_1t::run),
39	    (ice_ps_2t::NAME, ice_ps_2t::run),
40	    (ice_rr_1t::NAME, ice_rr_1t::run),
41	    (ice_rr_2t::NAME, ice_rr_2t::run),
42	    (zcr_with_1t::NAME, zcr_with_1t::run),
43	    (zcr_with_2t::NAME, zcr_with_2t::run),
44	];
45	
46	/// All registered bench names, in [`REGISTRY`] order. Used for CLI
47	/// help and the `all` resolution.
48	pub fn names() -> Vec<&'static str> {
49	    REGISTRY.iter().map(|(n, _)| *n).collect()
50	}
51	
52	/// Resolve a list of CLI-requested names (or the literal `"all"`)
53	/// to an ordered list of [`RunFn`]s. A name that matches no bench
54	/// exactly runs every bench it is a prefix of (`ice` → all four
55	/// ice benches, `mpsc` → both mpsc benches), in [`REGISTRY`]
56	/// order. Returns an error on any name matching nothing.
57	pub fn resolve(requested: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<RunFn>, String> {
58	    if requested.iter().any(|n| n == "all") {
59	        return Ok(REGISTRY.iter().map(|(_, run)| *run).collect());
60	    }
61	
62	    let mut runners = Vec::with_capacity(requested.len());
63	    for name in requested {
64	        if let Some((_, run)) = REGISTRY.iter().find(|(n, _)| n == name) {
65	            runners.push(*run);
66	            continue;
67	        }
68	        let prefixed: Vec<RunFn> = REGISTRY
69	            .iter()
70	            .filter(|(n, _)| n.starts_with(name.as_str()))
71	            .map(|(_, run)| *run)
72	            .collect();
73	        if prefixed.is_empty() {
74	            return Err(format!(
75	                "unknown bench '{name}'. valid: all, {}",
76	                self::names().join(", ")
77	            ));
78	        }
79	        runners.extend(prefixed);
80	    }
81	    Ok(runners)
82	}
83

  "old_string": "pub fn leak_ring() -> (Producer<'static>, Consumer<'static>) {\n    let region: &'static mut Region = Box::leak(Box::new(Region([0; REGION_BYTES])));\n    Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, CAPACITY)\n        .expect(\"geometry is valid by construction\")\n        .split()\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn leak_ring() -> (Producer<'static>, Consumer<'static>) {\n    let region: &'static mut Region = Box::leak(Box::new(Region([0; REGION_BYTES])));\n    Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, CAPACITY)\n        .expect(\"geometry is valid by construction\")\n        .split()\n}\n\n/// MPSC region bytes: the [`MpscHeader`] plus the per-slot seq\n/// array ([`CAPACITY`] × 4 B padded to a cache line) plus\n/// [`CAPACITY`] slots of one cache line each.\nconst MPSC_REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<MpscHeader>()\n    + (CAPACITY as usize * 4).next_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE)\n    + CACHE_LINE_SIZE * CAPACITY as usize;\n\n/// Cache-line-aligned backing region for one MPSC ring.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct MpscRegion([u8; MPSC_REGION_BYTES]);\n\n/// Build an MPSC ring over a leaked region and split it into\n/// `'static` endpoint handles — the MPSC sibling of\n/// [`leak_ring`], same leak rationale.\npub fn leak_mpsc_ring() -> (MpscProducer<'static>, MpscConsumer<'static>) {\n    let region: &'static mut MpscRegion = Box::leak(Box::new(MpscRegion([0; MPSC_REGION_BYTES])));\n    MpscRing::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, CAPACITY)\n        .expect(\"geometry is valid by construction\")\n        .split()\n}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/zcr_mpsc_1t.rs",
  "content": "//! Single-threaded zc-ring-x1 MPSC round-trip bench, closure\n//! (`send_with`) API.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{MpscConsumer, MpscProducer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, leak_mpsc_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-mpsc-1t\";\n\n/// Same-thread round-trip sending through the MPSC ring's\n/// `send_with` and receiving through its consumer guard.\n///\n/// - The wait closures never run here (one message in flight,\n///   never full/empty), so the measurement is the MPSC\n///   protocol's uncontended fast path — one claim CAS plus the\n///   per-slot seq publish, against `zcr-with-1t`'s\n///   load/store-only SPSC pair.\npub struct ZcrMpsc1Thread {\n    producer: MpscProducer<'static>,\n    consumer: MpscConsumer<'static>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrMpsc1Thread {\n    /// Construct the bench over one fresh leaked MPSC ring.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let (producer, consumer) = leak_mpsc_ring();\n        Self {\n            producer,\n            consumer,\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrMpsc1Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-mpsc-1t: zc-ring-x1 mpsc send_with round-trip (1 thread)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        let c = self.counter;\n        self.producer\n            .send_with::<Msg>(\n                |_| {\n                    core::hint::spin_loop();\n                    true\n                },\n                |m| *m = c,\n            )\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let slot = self\n            .consumer\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrMpsc1Thread::new();\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(\n        bench.name(),\n        outer,\n        inner,\n        duration_s,\n        &hist,\n        cfg,\n        suspended_s,\n    );\n}\n"
}

  "content": "//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 MPSC round-trip bench, closure\n//! (`send_with`) API, spin waits.\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::thread;\n\nuse zc_ring_x1::{MpscConsumer, MpscProducer};\n\nuse crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_mpsc_ring};\nuse crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};\nuse crate::pin;\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"zcr-mpsc-2t\";\n\n/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 MPSC\n/// rings — one producer per ring, so this is the \"MPSC when\n/// you don't need it\" number against `zcr-with-2t`'s SPSC\n/// pair at the same placement.\n///\n/// - Wait policy: a `spin_loop` hint per failed attempt on\n///   both the send and receive sides.\n/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker\n///   exits on receipt without replying.\npub struct ZcrMpsc2Thread {\n    req_tx: MpscProducer<'static>,\n    resp_rx: MpscConsumer<'static>,\n    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,\n    counter: u64,\n}\n\nimpl ZcrMpsc2Thread {\n    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked\n    /// MPSC rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.\n    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {\n        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_mpsc_ring();\n        let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_mpsc_ring();\n        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {\n            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);\n            loop {\n                let v = {\n                    let slot = req_rx\n                        .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                            core::hint::spin_loop();\n                            true\n                        })\n                        .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n                    let v = *slot;\n                    slot.release();\n                    v\n                };\n                if v == STOP {\n                    break;\n                }\n                resp_tx\n                    .send_with::<Msg>(\n                        |_| {\n                            core::hint::spin_loop();\n                            true\n                        },\n                        |m| *m = v,\n                    )\n                    .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n            }\n        });\n        Self {\n            req_tx,\n            resp_rx,\n            worker: Some(worker),\n            counter: 0,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Bench for ZcrMpsc2Thread {\n    fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        \"zcr-mpsc-2t: zc-ring-x1 mpsc send_with round-trip (2 threads, spin)\"\n    }\n\n    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {\n        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);\n        if self.counter == STOP {\n            self.counter = 1;\n        }\n        let c = self.counter;\n        self.req_tx\n            .send_with::<Msg>(\n                |_| {\n                    core::hint::spin_loop();\n                    true\n                },\n                |m| *m = c,\n            )\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let slot = self\n            .resp_rx\n            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                core::hint::spin_loop();\n                true\n            })\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        let v = *slot;\n        slot.release();\n        black_box(v)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Drop for ZcrMpsc2Thread {\n    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.\n    fn drop(&mut self) {\n        self.req_tx\n            .send_with::<Msg>(\n                |_| {\n                    core::hint::spin_loop();\n                    true\n                },\n                |m| *m = STOP,\n            )\n            .expect(\"spin closure never gives up\");\n        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {\n            let _ = worker.join();\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let mut bench = ZcrMpsc2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));\n    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);\n    harness::print_report(\n        bench.name(),\n        outer,\n        inner,\n        duration_s,\n        &hist,\n        cfg,\n        suspended_s,\n    );\n}\n"
}

```json
{
  "command": "vc-x1 push main \\\n  --title \"docs: mpsc iiac-perf numbers\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nWork 0.11.0-4: fresh 300s calibrated numbers for the four zcr\nbenches — the A/B the sibling-primitive split was for.\n\n- README.md: perf block replaced with the iiac-perf 0.19.0\n  run (zcr-with-1t/2t + the new zcr-mpsc-1t/2t); the shell\n  prompt lines are gone since this run came from the bot's\n  shell, the command line stays.\n- notes/todo.md: ladder markers advanced.\n- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: version-of-record 0.11.0-4.\n\nAdjusted means: with-1t 2.3 ns vs mpsc-1t 4.4 ns (+2.1 ns for\nclaim CAS + seq publish, uncontended); with-2t 100.1 ns vs\nmpsc-2t 73.9 ns — the MPSC protocol is *faster* cross-thread\nat 1p/1c. We think the SPSC round-trip bounces both index\nlines between cores (each side polls the other's line), while\nthe MPSC handoff communicates only through the slot's seq\nword and neither side ever reads the other's index line.\nmpsc-2t is also wider (p10 35.8 / p90 89.9 vs with-2t's tight\n88-98) and slightly heavier in the far tail.\n\nThe zcr-mpsc benches live in the sibling iiac-perf repo\n(zcr_mpsc_1t/2t + zcr_common::leak_mpsc_ring), uncommitted\nthere pending review. The 2p+1c contention and fan-in\ncomparator benches remain on the design's measurement plan —\nthe round-trip harness needs a shape decision for competing\nproducers first.\nEOF\n)\" \\\n  --yes 2>&1 | tail -3",

> AGENT

`-1..-4` are all landed and pushed. Summary for when you're back:

## The cycle so far (all pushed to main)

- **`-1` layout + init/attach** — `src/mpsc/` module gated on `target_has_atomic="32"`: `MpscHeader` (own magic `"ZCM1"`, layout v1), packed seq array between header and slots, capacity capped at 2³⁰ for tombstone headroom. Cross-kind attach fails `BadMagic` both directions (tested). Also filed **bug #1**: the pool's free-stack CAS is ungated, so thumbv6m already can't build the crate — pool/registry should gate like mpsc.
- **`-2` endpoints** — `MpscProducer` (Clone + Send + Sync, `&self` `send_with` closure API) with the claim CAS loop and a `TombstoneOnUnwind` guard (panic in fill publishes `pos+1+2³¹`; consumer releases without delivering — the queue can't wedge). `MpscConsumer` keeps the SPSC guard shape, CAS-free. 7 new tests incl. threaded 2-producer stress with per-producer FIFO asserted; **full Miri suite clean**.
- **`-3` demo** — mpsc lines beside spsc at every placement + the 2p+1c line. Eyeball: 1t mpsc 12.4 vs spsc 2.8 ns/msg.
- **`-4` calibrated numbers** — `zcr-mpsc-1t/2t` benches added to iiac-perf, 300s run in the README.

## The headline result

- **1t**: with 2.3 ns vs mpsc 4.4 ns — the claim CAS + seq publish costs ~2.1 ns uncontended, as expected.
- **2t**: with 100.1 ns vs **mpsc 73.9 ns — MPSC is *faster* cross-thread at 1p/1c.** We think the SPSC round-trip bounces both index cache lines (each side polls the other's line), while the MPSC handoff communicates only through the slot's seq word — neither side ever reads the other's index line. The mpsc distribution is wider, though (p10 35.8 / p90 89.9 vs SPSC's tight 88–98).

That result may be worth a design-doc note — it suggests the seam-word idea could even feed back into SPSC someday.

## Awaiting your direction

1. **Close-out (`0.11.0`)** — not delegated, so I stopped before it. It's bookkeeping: move the In Progress block into chores, Done entry, `notes/README.md` check, squash decision at push.
2. **iiac-perf changes are uncommitted** in the sibling repo (2 bench files, `leak_mpsc_ring`, registry, lockfile bump to `0.11.0-3`) — review and commit there at your leisure.
3. **Deferred from `-4`**: the 2p+1c contention bench and the fan-in comparator need a harness-shape decision (the round-trip harness doesn't naturally express competing producers) — they stay on the design's measurement plan.

> DEVELOPER

Is the mean of 73.9ns for zcr-mpsc-26 is a mean of the message count, if so there are two paths to the consumer and it's able to process the two streams with less spinning. Just a guess.

> AGENT

Good question to poke at — but first, what the mean actually averages: in that harness each `step()` is one **complete round-trip** (send request into ring A → worker echoes into ring B → receive reply), with `inner=1` meaning every step is timed individually. The 73.9 ns is the mean over the ~2.34 B round-trip samples (adjusted = minus the 11.61 ns timing framing). There is exactly **one message in flight at any moment** — strict ping-pong.

And on topology: `zcr-with-2t` and `zcr-mpsc-2t` are deliberately identical shapes — **both** use two rings (request + response), one producer and one consumer per ring. So there's no "two streams" concurrency in the mpsc run that the spsc run lacks; nothing overlaps in either. Whatever the 26 ns is, it's per-handoff protocol behavior, not stream parallelism.

The mechanism we pointed at in the commit body (marked as a guess there too): count the cross-core cache lines in one handoff's critical path —

- **SPSC**: the producer must *load* `consumer_idx` (fullness check) — a line the consumer writes; the consumer spins *loading* `producer_idx` — a line the producer writes. Two distinct index lines bouncing between the cores, each written by one side and polled by the other, every message.
- **MPSC**: the producer's CAS on `producer_idx` is **core-local** here (it's the only producer, and the consumer never reads that line); `consumer_idx` is never read by the producer. The only shared hot word is the slot's **seq** — one seam line instead of two index lines. (With capacity 8 and packed 4-byte seqs, all eight seq words even share a single cache line.)

Your instinct that it's "less spinning" may still be right in a narrower sense — the consumer spins on the seq word, which is also exactly the word the producer stores, whereas the SPSC consumer's spin and the producer's fullness check hit *different* lines and both must make the trip. The mpsc distribution is consistent with something interesting: 1% of round-trips complete in ~18 ns adjusted and p10 is 35.8, while with-2t never dips below ~47 — mpsc sometimes catches a much cheaper handoff, but with more variance (p90 89.9, heavier far tail).

All of this is We-think territory. Two cheap ways to actually verify, if you want them queued:

1. **perf counters** — run both benches under `perf stat` with cache-to-cache transfer events (`mem_load_l3_hit_retired.xsnp_hitm` equivalents on Zen 2); the two-lines-vs-one hypothesis predicts a measurably higher HITM rate for with-2t.
2. **A padded-seq variant** — if the effect were the packed seq line, padding changes it; if it's the index lines, padding doesn't. (Already an open question in the design doc.)

Worth capturing this discussion as a chores note at close-out either way. Want me to run the perf-counter comparison, or park both as a todo?

> DEVELOPER

Let's just add a note that the mean measures better in the mspc case and we should explore why and maybe we'll be able to improve spsc.
Then let's close out the change.

> AGENT

Adding the exploration note as a Todo, then closing out. First the note:

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/chores/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "## feat: mpsc ring sibling primitive\n\nCommits:\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "## feat: mpsc ring sibling primitive\n\nCommits:\n\nN senders sharing one queue needs a multi-producer ring, and\nthe SPSC hot path (load/store-only, ~2 ns 1t) must not pay\nfor it — so MPSC lands as a separate sibling primitive,\nmeasured A/B against SPSC in iiac-perf. Design:\n[MPSC ring (sibling primitive)](../ring-buffer-design.md#mpsc-ring-sibling-primitive).\n\n- Vyukov's bounded MPMC restricted to MP/SC:\n  - producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`;\n  - a per-slot seq array publishes each slot independently\n    (claim order ≠ commit order under concurrency);\n  - the consumer stays CAS-free, and producers never read\n    `consumer_idx` — fullness comes from the slot seq.\n- Closure send (`send_with`) makes claim abandonment\n  unrepresentable; a panicking fill publishes a tombstoned\n  commit (`pos + 1 + 2^31`) the consumer releases without\n  delivering — an unwind cannot wedge the queue.\n- Own magic (`\"ZCM1\"`) and layout version; the module is\n  gated on `target_has_atomic = \"32\"`. Filing the gate found\n  bug #1: the pool's free-stack CAS is ungated, so\n  load/store-only targets already fail to build.\n- Demo grew mpsc lines at every placement plus the 2p+1c\n  shape; iiac-perf grew `zcr-mpsc-1t`/`zcr-mpsc-2t` (bench\n  code in the sibling repo, committed there separately).\n- Deferred to the design's measurement plan: the 2p+1c\n  contention bench and the fan-in comparator — the\n  round-trip harness needs a competing-producer shape\n  first.\n\n### Outcome: the 2t surprise\n\n300s adjusted means: `zcr-with-1t` 2.3 ns vs `zcr-mpsc-1t`\n4.4 ns — the uncontended claim CAS + seq publish costs\n~2.1 ns, as expected. Cross-thread at 1p/1c the sign flips:\n`zcr-mpsc-2t` 73.9 ns vs `zcr-with-2t` 100.1 ns — the MPSC\nprotocol measures *faster*, with a wider spread (p10 35.8 /\np90 89.9 vs SPSC's tight 88–98). We think the SPSC\nround-trip bounces two index cache lines per handoff (each\nside polls the line the other writes) while the MPSC\nhandoff's only shared hot word is the slot seq — neither\nside ever reads the other's index line. Promoted to a\n`## Todo` entry (explore via perf cache-transfer counters\nand/or the padded-seq variant; if confirmed, a seam-word\nvariant might feed back into the SPSC protocol).\n\n### As-built ladder\n\n- `0.11.0-0` docs: mpsc ring design + plan\n- `0.11.0-1` feat: mpsc ring layout + init/attach\n- `0.11.0-2` feat: mpsc send_with + consumer recv\n- `0.11.0-3` feat: demo mpsc flow lines\n- `0.11.0-4` docs: mpsc iiac-perf numbers\n- `0.11.0` feat: mpsc ring sibling primitive\n\n# References"
}
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores/chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; as a chores file grows
9	large the next one is opened ([chores/chores-01.md](chores/chores-01.md)
10	is the active one), and earlier ones are closed and
11	kept for history.
12	
13	Project design docs:
14	
15	- [ring-buffer-design.md](ring-buffer-design.md) — the
16	  zero-copy ring buffer (requirements, layout, API,
17	  validation), kept in sync with `src/`.
18	
19

22	# Chores-01.md

26	how and why of a particular chore.

37	[`../AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md):
38	
39	- [Notes file conventions](../AGENTS.md#notes-file-conventions)
40	  — Todo format, Reference numbering, Notes references
41	  (`[[N]]` citation style), Markdown anchor links, Retiring
42	  Done entries.
43	- [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions) —
44	  section headers / Done entries exact-title rule, content
45	  rules, `Commits:` line format.
46	- [Code Conventions](../AGENTS.md#code-conventions) — doc
47	  comments, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
48	  stuck detection.
49	
50	Per-cycle workflow lives in
51	[`cycle-protocol.md`](cycle-protocol.md):
52	
53	- [Cycles](cycle-protocol.md#cycles) — three-phase shape
54	  (Preparation → Work → Close-out), `X.Y.Z-N` numbering,
55	  sub-cycles.
56	- [Per-commit flow](cycle-protocol.md#per-commit-flow) —
57	  cargo cycle (`fmt` / `clippy` / `test` / `install`),
58	  work + commit description review gates.
59	- [Commit description](cycle-protocol.md#commit-description)
60	  — Conventional Commits title rules (no version suffix,
61	  distinct per step); body shape per app vs `.claude` repo.

```
- `harness.rs` and `probe.rs` each grew a `band_index` helper (the
  membership test was duplicated at two sites per file — the band
  histogram pass and the trimmed-variance pass); both now call it, so
  the `<` → `<=` change lives in one place per file, each with a unit
  test pinning the boundary-exact cases.
- Only ranks landing *exactly* on a boundary move (down one band, into
  the capped band); every strictly-interior rank is unchanged, so
  normal multi-sample runs are unaffected — visible only in
  tiny/degenerate runs.

### Band membership

The mechanics — the Hazen `mid_rank = (i − 0.5) / n` formula, the
right-closed `(lower, upper]` convention (with the open/closed
definition and why it fits this report's upper-boundary labels), the
worked 10-value and single-sample tables, and the
interval-convention citations (Hazen, pandas, numpy, Dijkstra
EWD831) — live in the README's
[Reading a report](/README.md#reading-a-report) section, the single
source of truth. This note records only the decision to adopt
right-closed bands so a boundary-exact rank (a lone median sample)
reads `p50`; see the README for the full explanation.

## docs: add "Reading a report" to README

Commits:

The report format was described under `## Usage`, but there was no
practical "how to read a row and poke at it" guide, and the
band-membership mechanics (the Hazen rank formula, the right-closed
interval convention, the citations) lived only in the
upper-closed-intervals chores note above — history, not where a user
looks. Promote them to a user-facing README section and make that the
single source of truth.

- New README `### Reading a report` (top of `## Example runs`): a
  column key, the whole-histogram-vs-trimmed distinction, the
  `mid_rank` → band mapping (Hazen formula + right-closed
  `(lower, upper]` with an explicit open/closed definition), the
  worked 10-value and single-sample tables, the interval-convention

# Todo

This file uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
contains near term tasks with a short description and
uses links or reference links for more details.

## In Progress

When a `## Todo` item is picked up, its text moves here: the
problem overview and its list of things to do. That is followed
by the "plan" — a bulleted list of the development "ladder":
   - 0.xx.y-0 blah (done)
   - 0.xx.y-1 blah blah (current)
   - 0.xx.y-2 blah blah blah
   - 0.xx.y close-out and validation

_No cycle currently in progress._

## Todo

Entries are in **strict priority rank** — #1 highest,
descending. Reprioritize by moving an entry, then
`vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/todo.md` to renumber.
The numbers are positional rank, not stable IDs — to refer
to a Todo, name it by its **title** (a greppable mention;
a numbered list item has no anchor to link to), not its
number. Use the
[Prose Form in AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#prose-form); deeper
detail goes in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` design
subsections (link via `[N]` ref).

1. Unit scaling in report columns (`us`/`ms`) — per-row
   auto-scale so columns stay eyeball-comparable (bands are
   monotonic, so a row's first/last/mean share a magnitude),
   or `--units ns|auto` for script-stable output; needs
   `--decimals` landed first (`3.18 ms` vs `3 ms`); candidate
   `-4` for the report-options cycle.
2. Investigate: suspend gap missing from samples. A 0.13.5
   `--no-inhibit` suspend test detected ~1.2 s suspended inside
   the measured window but the max sample was only 4.0 ms,

We think\n  SPSC bounces two index cache lines per handoff while MPSC's\n  only shared hot word is the slot seq; the exploration is\n  tracked in zc-ring-x1's todo.\n- Cargo.lock: the zc-ring-x1 git dep advances to 0.11.1 (the\n  MPSC release + backfill tip).\n\n# References"
